How to read this document

  • Section 1–6 set the business case, the scope and the module split against the existing platform.
  • Section 7 holds one chapter per module that is built (7.1–7.11) plus the cross-cutting shell capabilities (7.12): purpose, scope, business rules, numbered functional requirements, user stories with acceptance criteria, data, integrations, reports and screens.
  • Section 8 holds the analytics-only pages that sit over existing platform modules.
  • Section 9 lists, per existing platform module, what SynapseDC reads from it, sends to it and through which interface.
  • Sections 10–15: data, non-functional requirements, phasing, risks and open decisions, traceability, glossary.
  • Section 17: how the application is generated (Singularity) from this BRD and the ideation code: UI reference, libraries, screen-to-code map, what not to generate, mock-to-live and acceptance rules.
  • Section 9.11–9.25: the expected request and response of every API SynapseDC consumes from the existing modules. Appendix B: every screen built, tab by tab and feature by feature, with its requirement ids, data source and phase.

Requirement identifiers: <PFX>-nnn (functional), BRULE-<PFX>-nn (business rule), US-<PFX>-nn (user story), ARCH-nnn (build boundary), NFR-nnn, INTG-nnn, DATA-nnn, GEN-nnn (generation rule). Priority uses MoSCoW: M must, S should, C could. Model: Both, Colo (colocation), Neo (NeoCloud / GPU cloud). Phase: P1 MVP, P2, P3 (section 12). Anything the prototype does not yet decide is marked (to discuss).

1Executive summary

Datacenter operators run their estate on separate tools: a BMS for the building, an NMS for the network, a DCIM for records, a ticketing tool for incidents, spreadsheets for capacity and billing. None of them answers the questions that matter in the first minutes of a fault: what is failing, who is affected, what is it costing, and what should we do first? For a GPU cloud the gap is sharper still: a single failed GPU can stop a 152-GPU training job, and nothing today joins the facility, the fabric, the GPU and the job into one picture.

SynapseDC closes that gap on top of the platform the customer already runs. It adds the datacenter-specific capabilities the platform does not have — the live facility (power, cooling, environment), the physical last mile (which UPS feeds which rack, which cable carries which service), the GPU fleet and its jobs, datacenter alarm scope and correlation, the revenue impact of every open issue, capacity and power economics, a planner for new capacity, DCIM records for racks, power chains and cabling, datacenter security operations and compliance — and it uses the platform's existing modules for everything they already own: CRM, customers, orders and catalogue (Operate to Acquire / BSS), incidents, changes, tickets, maintenance and knowledge (Services & ITSM), alerts, telemetry and security tooling (Opstral Ops Toolkit), AI models (AI Foundry), customisation (Platform Engineering), the data layer (Intelligence Hub) and inventory, IP management, programs and projects (Plan to Build).

First release targets two business models: colocation (the operator sells power, space and cooling; tenant equipment is never looked into) and NeoCloud / GPU cloud (the operator rents GPUs and must keep expensive jobs running). Both share one estate model, one alarm model and one money model; what differs is vocabulary, what is monitored and how value is counted. Cloud, enterprise and telco variants follow.

Headline outcomes

OutcomeMeasureTarget
Faster, better-ordered responseTime from first alarm to an incident with impact and a recommended action≤ 60 s automated; ordered by money and customers at risk
Less revenue lostRevenue impact of open issues is visible, reconciled and reducedEvery open issue carries lost-to-date, credits exposure and forecast; Σ issues = Σ customers = Σ sites
GPU productive timeGPU time lost (stalled, slowed, idle allocations)Visible per job, customer and site within one polling interval; work-to-redo from the last checkpoint
Fewer surprises from the buildingSingle points of failure, ride-through, cooling spareEvery live site has an SPOF list, a what-if and a ride-through figure
Capacity sold with confidenceNet available power / space after holds; power contract findingsReservations and contract findings on every site
One record of the physical estateRacks, power chain, cabling as records under change controlEvery DCIM write carries a change request

2Business context

2.1Problem statement

  1. Fragmented view. Facility, network, compute, GPU, security and commercial data live in different tools with different identities. Nobody can see one site end to end.
  2. Impact is guessed. When a UPS goes on battery or a backhaul link drops, the services and customers affected — and the money at risk — are worked out by hand, after the event.
  3. GPU operations are blind to the building. GPU clouds see Xid errors and job states, but not that the CDU flow dropped or a rack was power-capped. Facility teams see the CDU, not the job.
  4. Capacity decisions are slow. Where to place the next 256 GPUs, what a new hall costs, when power runs out — answered with spreadsheets and long cycles.
  5. The physical last mile is undocumented. Which PSU is on which PDU outlet, which trunk carries which service, which cabinet is single-fed: unknown until a fault proves it.
  6. Power is the largest cost and the least managed. Take-or-pay floors, demand ratchets and idle colocation blocks are found at invoice time.

2.2Goals

IDGoalHow the solution meets it
G-01One operational picture of every datacenterCommand Center and site page: health, areas, alarms in scope, incidents, impact, money
G-02Impact and cost of every open issue, reconciledRevenue Impact model charged to one issue per service, Σ across every view
G-03Keep GPU jobs runningGPU Fleet: per-GPU telemetry, jobs, blast radius, GPU time lost, placement
G-04Make the building resilient on paper before it failsPhysical last mile, SPOFs, what-if, ride-through, cooling spare, maintenance
G-05Plan and build capacity fasterCapacity, power economics, Planner with templates, benchmarks, governance, plan pack; hand-off to Programs & Projects
G-06Keep the estate's records rightDCIM under change control, lifecycle, reconciliation with the inventory module
G-07Secure and compliant operationsDatacenter Security Ops on ATT&CK / 800-61 / 62443; compliance against ISO 27001, ISO 22237, TIA-942, SOC 2
G-08Reuse the platformIntegrate with every existing module; build only what is datacenter-specific

2.3Success criteria for the first release

  • A colocation operator and a NeoCloud operator run the Operate core (section 12, P1) on at least one live site each, with the collector connected to the BMS, UPS, PDUs, network, BMCs and (NeoCloud) DCGM and the scheduler.
  • Every open incident at those sites shows affected services, customers, premium customers and money; the totals reconcile across Command Center, site, customer and issue views.
  • GPU jobs at the NeoCloud site show state, MFU, last checkpoint and GPU time lost; a stalled job raises a Critical incident within 60 s of the Xid.
  • DCIM holds every rack, PDU, cord and cable of the pilot sites; every write carries a change request from the existing Change Management module.

3Scope and the module split

3.1Principle

SynapseDC consumes the existing platform modules only through their APIs. It builds no database, backend service or screen for any of them. It builds only the datacenter-specific screens and functionality listed in section 7, and telemetry reaches it only through the OAP telemetry ingestion / consumer service, which reads and writes ClickHouse. Section 3.4 states this as requirements.

The platform module that already owns an object is its system of record. SynapseDC builds only what is datacenter-specific on top of it, uses the module's data through its API or the platform event bus, and never duplicates its screens. Where a customer runs their own system instead of the platform module (ServiceNow, Salesforce, NetBox…), the same datacenter capabilities integrate with that system.

3.3Out of scope (all releases unless re-decided)

  • Control of any plant, BMS point, network configuration, BMC, security equipment or tenant equipment. SynapseDC reads; people act through a change request.
  • Billing and invoicing (the customer's billing system; the platform has no billing module). SynapseDC sends metered usage and credits.
  • Video storage, badge issuing, visitor check-in (the PACS / VMS / visitor systems; SynapseDC reads events and references).
  • Discovery, drift and reconciliation UI (the inventory module's job; SynapseDC reports what it observed).
  • A tenant portal (embed or the tenant-scoped API only, section 9 and NB-12).
  • Network service provisioning, orders and the service catalogue (BSS / OSS).

3.4Build boundary: consume the platform, build only what is datacenter-specific

The existing modules named in section 3.2 (Operate to Acquire / BSS except Revenue Impact; Services & ITSM; Opstral Ops Toolkit; AI Foundry; Platform Engineering; Intelligence Hub except Command Center; Plan to Build Inventory, Inventory Planning, IP Management and Programs & Projects; Supply Chain) are assumed to expose the APIs SynapseDC needs. SynapseDC calls those APIs; it does not re-create the module.

IDRequirementPriority
ARCH-001SynapseDC shall read and write the data of an existing platform module only through that module's published API (REST, GraphQL or events). It shall assume the API is available and list every field it needs per module (section 9).M
ARCH-002SynapseDC shall not build a database, schema, table, backend service or administration screen for an object an existing module owns (customers, contracts, orders, products, incidents, problems, changes, tickets, maintenance windows, knowledge articles, alerts, inventory assets, IP addresses, capacity plans, programmes, projects, work orders, purchase orders, vendors, contracts, stock, users, roles, custom fields).M
ARCH-003SynapseDC shall not keep a second copy of a module's records as its own record. It may cache API responses for performance (with the module's id and an expiry) and may store derived results keyed by the module's ids (for example impact or money per incident).M
ARCH-004Where SynapseDC needs a field a module API does not offer, the gap shall be raised to the module team as an API requirement (the API gap list in section 9.10). It shall not be worked around with a local table.M
ARCH-005SynapseDC shall build only the datacenter-specific screens, read models and the backend that serves them (chapters 7.1–7.12 and the analytics pages of chapter 8).M
ARCH-006Screens of an existing module shall not be rebuilt. SynapseDC links or deep-links to them (for example an incident opens in Services & ITSM, a project in Programs & Projects), or embeds them where the module supports it. The analytics pages of chapter 8 read the module's API and drill down into the module's own screens.M
ARCH-007Every write SynapseDC makes to an existing module (create an incident, raise a change request, hand a plan over as a project, post a worklog line, send a notice) shall go through that module's API, so the module applies its own rules, approvals and audit.M
ARCH-008All telemetry (metrics and time series from the collector: network, BMC, UPS, PDU, meters, BMS, DCGM, scheduler, fabric) shall be written through the OAP telemetry ingestion microservice and read through the OAP telemetry consumer microservice. These services read and write ClickHouse. SynapseDC shall run no time-series database, ingestion pipeline or telemetry store of its own.M
ARCH-009SynapseDC read models (live facility state, GPU fleet, physical last mile, impact, revenue, capacity, power economics) shall query recent and historical telemetry through the OAP consumer API (latest value, range, roll-up, top-N). They shall not query ClickHouse directly.M
ARCH-010Alarms and events shall be raised into, and read from, Ops Toolkit System Alerts through its API. Security telemetry and detections shall be read from Ops Toolkit Security Ops (its ClickHouse / OCSF tables) through its API.M
ARCH-011Identity, roles, scopes, feature flags and custom fields shall come from Platform Engineering. SynapseDC keeps no user store.M
ARCH-012SynapseDC shall keep its own storage only for datacenter-specific data that no platform module owns. The list is in the table below; anything else is consumed.M

5Business models

One estate, one data model, one set of screens; the persona changes vocabulary, what is monitored and how value is counted. First release: Colocation and NeoCloud. Later variants: Cloud, Enterprise, Network / telco (the prototype already carries them; they are not written as requirements here).

AspectColocationNeoCloud (GPU cloud)
What is soldCabinets, cages, suites: contracted kW, space, cross-connects, remote handsReserved GPU clusters and on-demand GPUs; jobs billed per GPU-hour
Customer word / service wordTenant / cabinet, cage, cross-connectCustomer / GPU cluster, job
What is monitored inside the rackNothing. Tenant gear is an opaque block: power at the cabinet (A / B draw vs contracted kW), inlet temperature and humidity, cage doorEverything the operator owns: node BMC, per-GPU DCGM telemetry, fabric rails, scheduler job metadata
NeverPolling or looking inside tenant equipmentWorkload data, model weights, code, job contents
Premium tierPremium tenants (SLA 99.999 / 99.99)Reserved customers (SLA 99.9 / 99.5)
Capacity unitkW and cabinetsGPUs
Money modelMonthly charge per service (cabinet / cage); a tenant above contracted power is not a loss; SLA credits on power / cooling / cross-connect availabilityGPU rate × GPUs; a stalled job loses the whole allocation, a slowed job the step-time share; credits on cluster availability
Operator-owned equipmentMeet-me room network, one operator rack per site (BMS/EPMS, access control, NVR, DCIM, monitoring servers)Border routers, firewalls, InfiniBand spine/leaf, Ethernet front-end, OOB, GPU nodes (DGX / HGX / OEM), storage, CPU hosts
Killer use case"Which tenants are riding a critical fault right now, and what will the credits be?""One failed GPU stopped 152 GPUs: which job, which customer, what is it costing per hour, where is the last checkpoint?"
Security focusPhysical: cages, mantraps, tailgating, badge review; operator networkOperator fabric and GPU nodes: crypto-mining on idle nodes, rogue OOB devices, BMC credentials, UPS network cards
Power economicsColocation blocks per committed kW-month; idle block cost; tenant overageUtility take-or-pay floor, demand charge ratchet; power per sold GPU-hour vs price

6.2Architecture

  • On-site collector (one per datacenter, active + standby) reads the equipment read-only (SNMPv3, gNMI, syslog, Redfish, IPMI, Modbus, BACnet / OPC UA, DCGM, scheduler, UFM, PACS), normalises alarms, buffers and sends outbound only over mTLS 443 (interface spec INT-01…13, INT-28).
  • Telemetry: the collector writes every metric and time series to the OAP telemetry ingestion microservice; SynapseDC reads it back through the OAP telemetry consumer microservice. Both read and write ClickHouse. SynapseDC has no telemetry store of its own (ARCH-008, ARCH-009).
  • Existing platform modules: consumed only through their APIs and events (ARCH-001…007). SynapseDC adds no database, backend or screen for them.
  • SynapseDC backend: the datacenter-specific read models (impact, revenue, RCA, GPU, physical, capacity, power economics, security, compliance), the API (REST v1 — the same contract the screens use), the event bus (CloudEvents on webhooks / Kafka) and API clients for the platform modules and customer systems (NB-01…12, EW). Its own storage holds only the datacenter-specific data listed in section 3.4.
  • Screens run in the platform shell or embedded in the portal (shell=false) with the platform's identity (OIDC).
  • Data: every count is derived from entities; nothing is typed in. Every sample keeps source and receipt time; fresh = younger than two polling intervals.

6.3Key design decisions (carried from the prototype)

IDDecision
DD-01An alarm counts against a datacenter only when in scope: site, edge (counted at both ends), dependency; downstream is never counted, only shown as a symptom under the causing incident.
DD-02Every impacted service is charged to exactly one issue (its most severe cause); money reconciles across issues, causes, datacenters, customers and sites.
DD-03Health is shown with LEDs, beacons, halos and chips; cabinets and plant stay neutral. Red / amber only for faults.
DD-04Every DCIM write runs under a change request: standard (pre-approved record work), an open CR at the site, or raise new.
DD-05The platform module owns the record; SynapseDC lists only the data it needs and never invents the module's fields or APIs.
DD-06Southbound is read-only and outbound-only from the site.
DD-09Existing platform modules are consumed only through their APIs; SynapseDC never builds a database, backend or screen for them (3.4).
DD-10Telemetry goes in through the OAP telemetry ingestion service and comes out through the OAP consumer service, on ClickHouse. SynapseDC runs no time-series store.
DD-07Entity identities are stable strings; every entity is seeded / keyed on its own id so adding data never reshuffles the rest.
DD-08Money is held in the operator's currency with the geography's formatting; tariffs and benchmarks are real local values marked illustrative until the customer's own replace them.

7Modules built by SynapseDC

Each chapter follows the same layout: purpose · scope · business rules · functional requirements · user stories and acceptance criteria · data · integrations · reports and KPIs · screens.

7.1Command Center

Purpose. One landing screen that tells the operations lead, in under a minute, how healthy the whole datacenter estate is, which sites are in trouble, who is affected and what to do next. Every figure on it is clickable and opens the list that produced it, with the same count. Lifecycle stage: Operate (with a Plan & Build strip for the build pipeline). Primary users: NOC / operations manager, datacenter site manager, head of operations, executive sponsor. Builds on: new (the existing Intelligence Hub keeps its own dashboards; this Command Center is datacenter-specific). Reads incidents from Services & ITSM, alarms from Ops Toolkit System Alerts, customers from BSS. Colocation: the first health area is Incident response; "premium customers" are premium tenants; tenant cages are not GPU fleet. NeoCloud: the first health area is GPU fleet (GPUs healthy and at full speed ÷ installed), job stalls drive the status, GPU time lost per hour is shown.

7.1.1Scope

  • In scope: estate health ring and verdict, four health areas, clickable headline numbers, live estate map, datacenter column (worst first), What is happening · Who is affected · Recommendations, Plan & build strip and build lens, info text on every widget.
  • Out of scope: incident and change records themselves (Services & ITSM Incidents / Change Management); customer master data (BSS CRM / Customers); generic platform dashboards (Intelligence Hub); revenue detail (7.6 Revenue Impact).

7.1.2Business rules

IDRule
BRULE-CC-01An alarm counts against a datacenter only when its scope is site, edge or dependency. Downstream alarms are never counted; they appear only as impact under the causing incident.
BRULE-CC-02Area score = good ÷ total × 100 (higher is better). The area status follows the worst single issue in it, not the percentage (one UPS on battery = Critical even at 98 %); the score bar is always neutral blue.
BRULE-CC-14What each area counts: Incident response = open incidents still within their resolution SLA ÷ open incidents; Power = UPS modules online + utility feeds normal ÷ (UPS modules + feeds); Cooling & environment = cooling units running + zones at or below 27 °C ÷ (units + zones); Network = physical links (NeoCloud: InfiniBand, Ethernet and OOB cables) up + devices reachable ÷ (links + devices).
BRULE-CC-03Operations health = the average of the four area scores.
BRULE-CC-04Area order: GPU fleet (or Incident response where no GPU estate) · Power · Cooling & environment · Network.
BRULE-CC-05GPU fleet: a GPU counts bad when it is in a stalled, slowed or at-risk job, failed, degraded or throttled; deliberate spares count good. Status = Critical when any job is stalled; Attention for slowed / at-risk jobs, idle allocations, throttling or failed GPUs.
BRULE-CC-06Verdict line = "N critical or high incidents at S of T datacenters", where N = Critical tile + High tile (a site may carry several).
BRULE-CC-07Every headline number opens a list filtered to exactly that number (Critical → open Critical incidents; High → open High; Past SLA → open incidents whose resolution SLA clock has run out; premium customers → the Who is affected panel). A site can carry several incidents, so the verdict's site count has an info bubble listing each site with its Critical + High counts.
BRULE-CC-08The datacenter column is sorted worst first by the worst open incident per site; quick filter All / Critical / High.
BRULE-CC-09The Network area's detail reads by plane in NeoCloud (InfiniBand · Ethernet · OOB cables down · nodes unreachable) and as physical links down · devices unreachable elsewhere; the score follows BRULE-CC-14 in both.
BRULE-CC-11Recommendations = the first pending or awaiting-approval action of every open Critical / High incident (Critical rank 1, High rank 2), every change pending approval ("Approve CHG…", emergency rank 1, else rank 3), a batch RMA when an incident's similar-failure analysis names other nodes on the same optics lot, and plan approvals waiting on the viewer; ranked, de-duplicated by title, top 5.
BRULE-CC-12What is happening = all open incidents across the estate, most severe first then by premium-customer impact, each with its resolution SLA clock (time left, red when overdue) and its services · customers · premium impact. Who is affected = services whose path crosses an open incident or active alarm, split Active / Degraded / Down / Disabled, and the exposed customers premium first (12 shown, total counted).
BRULE-CC-13A datacenter card shows its worst open incident (Critical red, High amber accent) or, with none, a quiet card: Attention (conditions to watch: active and critical alarms, degraded services) or Normal.
BRULE-CC-10Plan & build strip: plans by stage planned → approved → in build → commissioning → live, with decisions due.

7.1.4User stories and acceptance criteria

US-CC-01 As a NOC manager I want one verdict and four areas so that I know where to look first (CC-001, CC-002, CC-005).

  • Given a UPS is on battery at one site, when I open the Command Center, then Power shows Critical even if its score is above 95 % and the hover names the UPS.

US-CC-02 As a NOC manager I want every number to open its list so that I can trust the dashboard (CC-003, CC-004, CC-021).

  • Given the High tile shows 8, when I click it, then the incidents list opens filtered to open High incidents and shows 8 rows.

US-CC-03 As a NeoCloud operations lead I want GPU fleet first so that the product's health leads (CC-008, CC-010).

  • Given a training job is stalled by an Xid 79 GPU, then GPU fleet is Critical, the detail names the GPUs stopped and the money lost per hour, and the site chip opens that site's jobs.

US-CC-04 As a colocation site manager I want Incident response first so that tenant-facing response leads (CC-009).

  • Given a colocation estate with no operator GPUs, then the first area is Incident response with % within SLA.

US-CC-05 As an executive I want to see who is affected so that I can call key customers (CC-018).

  • Given three premium customers are affected, then the premium tile shows 3 and clicking scrolls to Who is affected with those three first.

US-CC-06 As an operations engineer I want the map to show where trouble is (CC-011, CC-012, CC-014).

  • Given a site has a Critical incident, then its pin ripples; when I search its city and pick it, the map flies to it and opens its card.

US-CC-07 As a planning lead I want the build pipeline on the landing page (CC-022, CC-023).

  • Given two plans await approval, then the strip shows 2 decisions due and Recommendations lists them for their approvers.

US-CC-08 As a platform user I want the screen embedded in the portal (CC-032).

  • Given the portal frames the Command Center with shell off, then no menu or top bar shows and links stay inside the frame.

US-CC-09 As a NOC manager I want to know what to do first (CC-035).

  • Given a Critical incident with a pending action and an emergency change awaiting approval, then Recommendations lists both at rank 1, each with its reason and benefit, and clicking opens the incident or the change.

US-CC-10 As a head of operations I want to ask the assistant (CC-031).

  • Given I ask "Which issue is costing the most revenue?", then the answer names the issue, its money per hour and links to it, and the figure equals the Revenue impact screen.

7.1.5Data

EntityRead / writeSystem of recordNotes
Datacenter (site)ReadSynapseDC DCIM / Plan to Build Inventorystate, tier, region, coordinates
AlarmReadOps Toolkit System Alerts (normalised by SynapseDC)scope, severity, category
IncidentReadServices & ITSM Incidents (enriched by SynapseDC)severity, SLA, impact
Customer, serviceReadBSS CRM / Customers / Orderspremium flag, SLA, monthly charge
GPU node, GPU, jobReadSynapseDC GPU Fleet (DCGM + scheduler)job state, GPU time lost
Revenue impactReadSynapseDC Revenue Impactlost, at risk
Plan, projectReadPlan to Build (Programs & Projects) / SynapseDC Plannerstage, decision due
Collector stateReadSynapseDC collectorup / stale / down

7.1.6Integrations

System / moduleDirectionDataInterface
Ops Toolkit System AlertsInnormalised alarmsEW, INT-28
Services & ITSM IncidentsInincident state, assigneeEW / NB-04
BSS CRM / CustomersIncustomers, premium, SLAEW / NB-07
Platform portalOutembedded screenNB-11
Event consumersOutincident / alarm / GPU job eventsNB-02, NB-03

7.1.7Reports and KPIs

  • Operations health % = mean(area scores).
  • GPU fleet % = GPUs healthy and at full speed ÷ GPUs installed.
  • Incident response % = open incidents still within their resolution SLA ÷ open incidents.
  • Power % = (UPS modules online + utility feeds normal) ÷ (UPS modules + feeds); Cooling & environment % = (cooling units running + zones ≤ 27 °C) ÷ (units + zones); Network % = (links up + devices reachable) ÷ (links + devices).
  • Sites with Critical or High incidents = count of distinct sites.
  • GPU time lost per hour = Σ (stalled job GPUs + slowed job GPUs × step-time share lost + idle allocated GPUs) × GPU rate.

7.2Datacenters — site page, layout / digital twin, access & security

Purpose. The datacenter page is the home of one site: its health, its plant and equipment, its layout in 3D, its customers and money at risk, who is inside the building, and its history. It turns an alarm into a place (floor → room → rack → device → port) and a consequence (services, customers, revenue). Lifecycle stage: Operate (with links to Plan and Build). Primary users: site manager, NOC engineer, facilities engineer, field technician, security officer, customer success. Builds on: Plan to Build Inventory (records), Ops Toolkit System Alerts, Services & ITSM, BSS Customers; the layout, twin, access log and impact views are new. Colocation: racks are tenant cabinets with owner and contracted kW; tenant gear is a sealed block (power and environment at the cabinet only, never polled); the Access tab shows tenants at their cage; Business impact lists tenants and cross-connect / remote-hands orders. NeoCloud: GPU Fleet sub tab, GPU nodes show the 8-GPU strip, rooms show liquid racks and CDUs, Business impact lists GPU customers and their jobs.

7.2.1Scope

  • In scope: Datacenters list (list and tiles), site header, four tab groups and their sub tabs, Needs attention, Metrics, Ops log, schematic and realistic layout views, walk mode, power and cable paths in 3D, device popup and traces, device page (overview, interfaces, hardware, Power & cabling, 3D view, GPUs · DCGM), Access & security tab, Business impact tabs.
  • Out of scope: incident workflow (Services & ITSM Incidents), customer records (BSS), the inventory master (Plan to Build Inventory, synced), badge administration (customer's PACS), video storage (customer's VMS).

7.2.2Business rules

IDRule
BRULE-SITE-01Tab groups are fixed: Overview (Datacenter layout · Needs attention · Metrics · Ops log) · Facility (Power & backup · Cooling · Environment · Access & security · Single points of failure · Maintenance & alarms) · Infrastructure (Equipment · GPU Fleet · Topology · Space & ports · Assets) · Business impact (Customers · Revenue). New tabs join a group; no fifth group.
BRULE-SITE-02The URL names the sub tab; the group follows from it. A group pill opens the sub tab last used in that group this session, else its first. Group count = sum of its sub tab counts.
BRULE-SITE-03GPU Fleet sub tab is shown only where the site has GPU infrastructure.
BRULE-SITE-04Device state: in-service (reporting), unmonitored (installed, no successful poll for 15 min), planned (on record, holds rack space only), tenant-owned (power and environment only).
BRULE-SITE-05A value is fresh while its last sample is younger than two polling intervals; older it is shown stale.
BRULE-SITE-06Layout attention: beacons on racks with Critical / Major issues; red / amber floor and room edges; issue counts on floor labels; at most 3 critical callouts, one per room. Cabinets stay neutral; health is shown by LEDs, beacons and halos.
BRULE-SITE-07Both layout views (schematic, realistic) use the same layout, attention roll-up, drill path and side panel, so numbers are identical.
BRULE-SITE-08Power path in 3D: per room an A (blue) and B (orange) busway, each fed by one UPS module; tripped tap-off = red dashed; on battery = amber dashed. Cable paths: trunk route 1 teal, route 2 purple, all links down red.
BRULE-SITE-09PSU cords: odd PSUs on PDU A, even on PDU B; Σ PDU load = rack draw.
BRULE-SITE-10Access: a door is "Held open" while an in-progress change names a unit in that room. Exceptions: tailgating, door forced / held open, anti-passback, repeated denials, after-hours entry, unescorted visitor, overstay, badge not returned.
BRULE-SITE-11Access log retention 1 year; CCTV references 90 days; the access-rights review is quarterly (badges by type, expired and unused badges).
BRULE-SITE-12Planned sites show an empty state on live-only tabs (no telemetry, no access log).
BRULE-SITE-13A figure shown on a Facility tab is not repeated on Metrics / Assets (single place per figure); the Metrics tab opens with the six-tile facility strip, each tile opening its sub tab.
BRULE-SITE-14Site condition: Critical = a Critical incident, a UPS on battery, a plant fault or an isolated edge; Needs attention = any open incident, critical alarm, link down, unreachable device or facility issue; else Normal. Edge redundancy = redundant / redundancy lost / isolated from the site's backhaul links.
BRULE-SITE-15Metrics thresholds: a zone is red above 27 °C and amber above 25.5 °C; a device chassis is red at 55 °C; device load is red when any device is at 90 % CPU; humidity band 40–60 % RH.
BRULE-SITE-16Diesel figures without a tank meter are estimates and shown as such: tank litres = DG kVA × 3.2 (rounded to 50 L), burn ≈ 0.2 L per kVA-hour at 75 % load, 30-day use from generator run hours. A tank reading replaces them.
BRULE-SITE-17Access log people: guards two per 12 h shift (07:00 / 19:00), field technicians per 12 h shift, site engineers on days, change crews escorted and tagged with their change, the on-call engineer responding to an open incident tagged with it, PM vendors, couriers, cleaners, visitors with host and escort, colocation tenants at their cage. Exception severities: door forced High; tailgating, held open, anti-passback Medium; badge not returned Low.
BRULE-SITE-18Rack record on Assets: type (a ~1 in 10 share of live non-GPU racks is an old shallow 600 × 1000 type), purchase date and condition (Damaged at audit, with the note); GPU / liquid racks are never the old type.

SITE-043The system shall mark on the schematic layout, when a rack is open, its facing row as glass with no clicks, and in the realistic view leave the facing row and cold-aisle frame out entirely.CBothP2
SITE-044The system shall place the realistic view's controls as: floor picker top-left, tools and a "?" help (mouse / keyboard) top-right, one dock bottom-left (Colour by · Power path / Cable paths · foldable legend Health · Power · Cables), path card bottom-right; labels keep clear of the dock.CBothP2
SITE-045The system shall render on the device 3D view one model per family (router, switch, firewall, optical, server, storage, GPU node, tenant block) with hardware parts coloured by kind (compute · power · cooling · network · memory · storage · management), state from modules, interfaces, GPU telemetry and rack power, cameras ¾ · Front · Rear · Top.SBothP2
SITE-046The system shall show the Space & ports tab's floors / rooms / racks table and rack space and port totals, and the datacenter Topology tab with the same view as the former topology page (/topology?dc= redirects to it).MBothP1
SITE-047The system shall keep every figure on the datacenter page reconciled with the Command Center and the lists (same incident, alarm, service and money counts).MBothP1

7.2.4User stories and acceptance criteria

US-SITE-01 As a NOC engineer I want to go from an alarm to its place so that I can send the right technician (SITE-010, SITE-011).

  • Given a Critical alarm on a rack PDU, when I open Datacenter layout, then a beacon marks that rack, its room edge is red and the attention card drills to the rack.

US-SITE-02 As a field technician I want to see which cable to check (SITE-019, SITE-027).

  • Given a link is down, when I open View cabling on the device, then every hop to the far device is listed and the check-first hop is marked.

US-SITE-03 As a facilities engineer I want to see what powers a device (SITE-017, SITE-019).

  • Given a device with PSU1 on PDU-A outlet 14, then the power trace shows PSU1 → PDU-A outlet 14 → busway A → its UPS module → utility feed, and a tripped outlet is red dashed.

US-SITE-04 As a colocation site manager I want tenant cabinets shown without touching tenant gear (SITE-022).

  • Given a tenant cabinet, then it shows owner, contracted kW and measured draw, its devices are one sealed block, and no device telemetry is requested.

US-SITE-05 As a NeoCloud engineer I want each GPU node's GPUs at a glance (SITE-023, SITE-029).

  • Given a node with one GPU raising Xid 48, then the strip shows that GPU in fault and the DCGM tab shows the row-remap and Xid fields.

US-SITE-06 As a security officer I want exceptions with context (SITE-030, SITE-031, SITE-033).

  • Given a door was held open during a change, then the exception links the CHG and the door shows Held open while the change is in progress.

US-SITE-07 As a site manager I want one place per figure (BRULE-SITE-13).

  • Given PUE is shown under Facility, then Metrics shows it only in the facility strip tile that opens Power & backup.

US-SITE-08 As a user without WebGL I still want the layout (SITE-020).

  • Given WebGL is unavailable, then a 2D view with the same attention items is shown.

US-SITE-09 As an account manager I want one page per customer (SITE-034, SITE-039, SITE-040).

  • Given a tenant with one cabinet on a tripped PDU-A, then the customer page verdict names that cause, "What is impacting" lists the issue with its money, and the cross-connect orders and remote-hands tickets are listed with their state.

US-SITE-10 As a head of operations I want the list to show where to look (SITE-001, BRULE-SITE-14).

  • Given a site with a UPS on battery and no open incident, then the list shows it as Critical; filtering Condition = Facility issue keeps it; filtering Clear hides it.

7.2.7Reports and KPIs

  • Devices monitored % = in-service ÷ (installed − tenant-owned).
  • MTTR 30 d = mean(resolved − detected) for incidents resolved in 30 days.
  • Rack inlet range = max − min inlet temperature over live racks.
  • Diesel runway h = fuel litres ÷ burn rate at current load.
  • Access exceptions 24 h, denied entries 24 h, visitors on site now.

7.3Facilities — power, cooling, environment & safety, single points of failure, maintenance

Purpose. Shows how power and cooling reach every rack, whether the site can ride through a utility failure, where one failure would take something down, and when the plant is next maintained. It is the facility engineer's working screen and the source of the facility figures used everywhere else. Lifecycle stage: Operate. Primary users: facilities (electrical, mechanical) engineer, site manager, NOC engineer, maintenance planner. Builds on: Services & ITSM Maintenance and Change Management (booking), Supply Chain Vendors / Contracts / Warehouses (support contracts, spares), Plan to Build Inventory (plant records); live state and the power-path model are new. Colocation: cabinet power vs contracted kW per tenant, A/B both-sides check per cabinet, tenant cages in access and fire zones; tenant gear is measured at the PDU only. NeoCloud: liquid-cooled GPU racks (40–60 kW+) with CDU loops, 75 % of liquid-rack heat to the CDU, CDU flow low → GPU slowdown story, GPU heat counts as Cooling.

7.3.1Scope

  • In scope: facility strip, Power & backup (power path, load and ride-through, cabinet distribution and what-if, rack power list, rack PDU outlet map), Cooling (heat balance, units, CDUs, coverage by floor, nearest unit), Environment (zones, fire, leak, doors), Single points of failure (+ resilience tests), Maintenance (PM plan, schedule maintenance), lifecycle / RMA / spares views.
  • Out of scope: controlling plant (never); maintenance work orders and CMMS records (Services & ITSM Maintenance / customer CMMS); vendor contracts master (Supply Chain Contracts); spares stock master (Supply Chain Warehouses); BMS configuration.

7.3.2Business rules

IDRule
BRULE-FAC-01Power chain: utility feed → ATS → UPS module (A side = UPS-2/4, B side = UPS-1/3) → busway per room → rack PDU A / B → breaker bank → outlet → PSU. Each PDU side is rated at the rack budget.
BRULE-FAC-02Odd PSUs connect to PDU A, even to PDU B; Σ PDU load = rack draw.
BRULE-FAC-03Ride-through sequence: UPS batteries (the shortest module's autonomy at current load) → the DG at 80 % of its kVA rating (kW) → fuel hours at the tested runtime (quoted at 60 % load). Readiness checks: UPS redundancy (every module online), utility feeds, DG test passed, battery test in date, fuel: ≥ 50 % OK, 30–50 % warning, < 30 % fault.
BRULE-FAC-04Cooling redundancy (heat balance): operating units ≥ units needed for the heat load + 1 when the design is N+1. Units on a floor share its air (one zone per floor). A room is hot when a unit in it is down or a sensor reads above 27 °C. A floor reads "N+1 holds", "No spare" (the design margin is lost and a unit is down, or it has a hot room) or "Short" (the site's running capacity is below its heat load and a unit is down).
BRULE-FAC-05Each rack has a nearest cooling unit; liquid racks send 75 % of their heat to their CDU.
BRULE-FAC-06Single points of failure: both cords on A, failed PSU, tripped PDU, cabinet that one side cannot carry (≥ 90 % of budget), UPS not online, Tier II single feed / tray route / building entry, cooling spare lost.
BRULE-FAC-07What-if assets: feed, UPS, side AB, PDU, cooling unit, trunk; result = devices, racks, services and customers that lose power, cooling or connectivity.
BRULE-FAC-08PM state: in progress / scheduled when a Maintenance change names the asset; overdue when next due < today; due within 7 days; upcoming within 30 days; else done. UPS inspection and fire inspection every 91 days.
BRULE-FAC-09Lifecycle state: End of support; Out of warranty (no warranty, no live contract); Expiring (contract ends < 90 days or end of support < 1 year); else Covered.
BRULE-FAC-10RMA cases come from modules in fault and GPU nodes flagged for RMA; an RMA never passes "Engineer booked" while the fault is live.
BRULE-FAC-11Leak detection: rope under cooling units, CDUs and liquid racks. Fire zone per room with detection (VESDA / point), suppression (Novec / IG-541 / pre-action), quarterly inspection.
BRULE-FAC-12PUE = facility power ÷ IT power from the site's power split (IT, cooling, lighting & other).
BRULE-FAC-14Rack power list: budget = the rating of one PDU side; Over budget at ≥ 85 % of budget, High at ≥ 75 %; Hot when the rack inlet is above 27 °C. The site power-load tile is red at ≥ 85 % of design capacity.
BRULE-FAC-15Environment safe band: 18–27 °C and 40–60 % RH per zone sensor; red = over temperature, smoke or leak; amber = humidity out of band. Moving to the ASHRAE dew-point envelope is to discuss.
BRULE-FAC-16Spares per part family: out when on hand − reserved ≤ 0, low when below the minimum, else OK. RMA status advances Opened → Part shipped → Engineer booked → Replaced · verifying, and never passes Engineer booked while the fault is live.
BRULE-FAC-17What-if on a utility feed drops nothing: the UPS and the DG carry it, so it is shown as a ride-through. What-if on a UPS takes every PDU it feeds; on a PDU its outlets; on a cooling unit its zone's spare; on a trunk every link in it.
BRULE-FAC-18PM intervals: UPS inspection and thermal scan, cooling filter and coil, CDU coolant and filter, fire inspection every 91 days; battery and DG tests on their own dates; a booked Maintenance change counts as scheduled, not overdue.
BRULE-FAC-13A closed section shows a one-line summary as status pills (red / amber / green); state remembered per viewer.

7.3.4User stories and acceptance criteria

US-FAC-01 As a facilities engineer I want to know how long the site lasts if utility power fails (FAC-005, FAC-006).

  • Given UPS-2 is on battery with 11 min left and the DG has not taken the load, then the ride-through panel shows a red note naming UPS-2 and 11 min.

US-FAC-02 As a facilities engineer I want to find racks at risk on one side (FAC-010, FAC-023).

  • Given a server has both cords on PDU-A, then it appears in Single points of failure as "both cords on A" with the rack and the fix.

US-FAC-03 As a colocation site manager I want tenants over their contracted power flagged (FAC-011).

  • Given a cabinet contracted at 8 kW draws 8.6 kW, then the rack power list shows 108 % and flags it; it is not counted as revenue loss.

US-FAC-04 As a NeoCloud engineer I want to see when cooling slows GPUs (FAC-016, FAC-017).

  • Given CDU flow is low at a liquid site, then the CDU card is in fault and links to the GPU jobs slowed by hardware slowdown.

US-FAC-05 As a maintenance planner I want to book plant maintenance (FAC-025, FAC-026).

  • Given a UPS inspection is overdue, when I click Schedule maintenance, then a Maintenance change is raised and the PM state becomes Scheduled.

US-FAC-06 As a site manager I want to test a failure before it happens (FAC-013).

  • Given I pick "side A" in what-if, then the system lists every device and service that would run on B only and those that would lose power.

US-FAC-07 As a procurement lead I want expiring support visible (FAC-028).

  • Given a contract ends in 60 days, then the device shows Expiring with the contract id and days left.

US-FAC-08 As an operator I want assurance the platform never controls plant (FAC-033).

  • Given any facility screen, then no control or set action is offered and the collector holds read-only credentials only.

US-FAC-09 As a mechanical engineer I want to know which floor has no cooling spare (FAC-018, BRULE-FAC-04).

  • Given a CRAH on floor 2 is down and the site's design is N+1, then floor 2 reads "No spare", its hot room is listed, and the heat balance shows capacity after losing the largest unit.

US-FAC-10 As a store keeper I want to know when spares run low (FAC-037).

  • Given the SFP spare's on hand minus reserved is below its minimum, then the spare reads Low and the open RMA for that part shows whether a spare is on site.

7.3.7Reports and KPIs

  • PUE = facility kW ÷ IT kW.
  • UPS shortest autonomy (min) = min(battery minutes at current load).
  • Ride-through h = DG fuel runtime at 60 % load.
  • Cooling margin kW = running capacity − heat load; after-loss kW = running − largest unit.
  • Rack budget use % = rack draw ÷ rack budget; cabinets ≥ 90 %.
  • SPOF count by domain; PM overdue count; devices Out of warranty / End of support.

7.4GPU Fleet

Purpose. Show every GPU cluster, node and customer job the operator runs. For each one, show whether it is healthy and earning, what is slowing or stopping it, and what that costs in GPU time, power and work to redo. This is the product's main use case: a GPU estate is the most expensive and the most fault-sensitive hardware in the building. Lifecycle stage: Operate (with Plan inputs for placement and power cost). Primary users: GPU / AI platform operations engineer, NOC engineer, facility engineer (liquid cooling), capacity planner, customer success manager, operations head. Builds on: new. It uses Opstral Telemetry Ops and Infra Ops pipelines for collection, and the BSS Customers / Orders records for job ownership and price.

Colocation: the GPUs belong to the tenant and are never polled. GPU Fleet shows only the tenant AI cages: high-density cabinets (40–60 kW), contracted kW against draw, the liquid loop (CDU) feeding them, inlet temperature and state. There are no jobs, nodes or DCGM telemetry. The menu item is shown only when the estate has GPU infrastructure. NeoCloud: full scope. Operator-owned GPUs, per-GPU DCGM-style telemetry, and customer jobs as the scheduler sees them (job, customer, GPUs and nodes, state, MFU, checkpoint). Workload data, models and code are never read.

7.4.1Scope

In scope

  • Clusters / pods (at most 32 nodes each, one GPU model per pod), nodes, GPUs and hot spares.
  • Jobs (training, fine-tuning, inference) with their states and the money effect of each state.
  • Per-GPU telemetry following the NVIDIA DCGM exporter fields, plus the Intel Gaudi (hl-smi) and Intel XPU Manager equivalents.
  • GPU models and vendors catalogue (NVIDIA B200, H200, H100, A100, L40S, T4; Intel Gaudi 3, Data Center GPU Max 1550).
  • Network rails: InfiniBand (400G NDR), Ethernet front-end (100G), Management / OOB (1G), with port and error state.
  • Cooling and power for GPU racks: liquid loops (CDU), rack draw against budget, power cost per GPU-hour, energy and power cost by job and by tenant.
  • Where to place the next job: a ranked list of pods.
  • "Jobs needing attention" story cards (biggest blast radius, slowest job).
  • Node detail and the device "GPUs · DCGM" view.

Out of scope

  • Job scheduling and submission: the customer's Slurm / Kubernetes / Run:ai.
  • Billing of GPU hours: the customer billing system. The BSS Plans & Products Catalog holds the GPU product and rate.
  • Ticketing and on-call: Services & ITSM Incidents and Tickets, Ops Toolkit System Alerts.
  • Model / training-framework internals, and any customer data.

7.4.2Business rules

IDRule
BRULE-GPU-01Job states are Running, Slowed, Stalled, At risk and Idle. Stalled means the job has stopped because one of its GPUs or nodes failed (for example Xid 79, GPU fell off the bus): every rank waits at the step barrier. Slowed means the step time is above the baseline (an NVLink straggler, thermal or hardware slowdown from low coolant flow, an InfiniBand rail flapping); MFU falls by the same factor. At risk means the job still runs but a node in it carries a fault where the next event crashes it (uncorrectable ECC with a row remap pending). Idle means the job holds an allocation but has run below 5 % utilisation for 6 hours or more; no alarm is raised for it (it is waste, not a fault) and it is shown on the jobs list and the Command Center GPU area instead. Job kinds are training, fine-tuning and inference; inference-only models (L40S, T4) run inference jobs only.
BRULE-GPU-02Job price per hour = GPUs × (persona rate per GPU-month ÷ 730) × the model's price factor (H100 = H200 = 1; B200 1.45, A100 0.55, L40S 0.38, T4 0.08, Gaudi 3 0.6, Max 1550 0.45). The NeoCloud reference rate is ₹1,50,000 per GPU-month (cloud list ₹1,75,000, enterprise charge-back ₹85,000 for the later variants); the customer's own product rate from the catalogue replaces it. The same figure is the job's service charge for Revenue Impact.
BRULE-GPU-03GPU time lost now (per hour) = the full job price per hour when Stalled or Idle; the job price per hour × the share of step time lost when Slowed (share = 1 − baseline ÷ current step time); 0 when Running.
BRULE-GPU-04Lost so far = the lost-now rate × hours since the state began. The fleet's "lost so far" total adds the work to redo of stalled jobs (BRULE-GPU-05).
BRULE-GPU-05Work to redo (Stalled job) = the job price per hour × hours between the last checkpoint and the stall.
BRULE-GPU-06At stake (At-risk job) = the job price per hour × the checkpoint interval in hours (the work lost if it crashes now).
BRULE-GPU-07Node states are In a job, Spare (idle, about 10 % of each pod kept as hot spares), Drained, Failed (open RMA; the failed GPU shows Xid 79 for NVIDIA parts) and Burn-in (24 h after a repair). A GPU counts as healthy and at full speed unless it is in a Stalled / Slowed / At-risk job, failed, degraded, or throttled. Spares count as healthy; drained, failed and burn-in nodes are out of service. Fleet health % = healthy ÷ installed; the Command Center GPU fleet area shows this figure with the detail "N GPUs stopped by one failed GPU · N jobs slowed · money lost per hour".
BRULE-GPU-08Fleet status is Critical when any job is Stalled. It is Attention when any job is Slowed or At risk, any allocation is Idle, any GPU is throttled, or any GPU has failed. Otherwise it is Normal.
BRULE-GPU-09Throttle reasons are taken from the DCGM throttle field and reported as thermal, hardware slowdown or power cap.
BRULE-GPU-10Xid buckets: 48, 64, 79 and 95 = reset the GPU / restart the node (Critical). 74 = NVLink (Major). 94 = contained (Minor). 63 = ignored.
BRULE-GPU-11Driver drift = a node running its model's older driver while the pod's baseline is the current driver.
BRULE-GPU-12Power cost per installed GPU-hour = kW per GPU × site PUE × energy tariff. Power cost per sold GPU-hour = the same power spread over GPUs that are earning (in a running job). Both are compared with the model's price to show power share % and "left after power".
BRULE-GPU-13Job / tenant power cost per day = Σ node kW × PUE × tariff × 24. An idle allocation is billed, draws power and does no work. A power cap reports kW saved against the GPU time it costs.
BRULE-GPU-14Placement fit for N GPUs of a model is judged per pod on: free healthy GPUs, rack power headroom, cooling state (CDU for liquid racks), contracted power headroom, "already paid" kW (an idle block, or demand below the take-or-pay floor) and the marginal power cost per GPU-hour. Fit is Good, Tight or No.
BRULE-GPU-15Each job is a customer service on every node of the job, so a fault on any of its nodes affects that service and appears in Revenue Impact under the GPU domain (see 7.6).
BRULE-GPU-16Telemetry is read-only. The platform never resets a GPU, drains a node or changes a power cap. It recommends these, and people carry them out through a change.
BRULE-GPU-17Pods are at most 32 nodes of 8 GPUs, one GPU model per pod; the GPU host takes the pod's server model (NVIDIA, Supermicro or Dell OEM). Liquid-cooled halls have one CDU loop per three GPU racks (capacity = load × 1.6 rounded up to 50 kW; nominal flow = capacity × 1.45 L/min); liquid nodes report no fan. Cluster state is bad when a job in it is stalled or a GPU is failed in a job, warn when any GPU needs attention, else ok.
BRULE-GPU-18Reference scenarios the acceptance tests use (seeded on NVIDIA training pods): Xid 79 stalls the biggest training job at the GPU-fault site, last checkpoint 52 min before; Xid 48 row remap pending puts a fine-tune At risk; Xid 74 NVLink errors make one node a straggler and the job 22 % slower; CDU flow low (pump A seized) puts liquid-cooled GPUs into hardware slowdown with a Cooling incident; an InfiniBand rail flapping slows a job 12 % with no incident on purpose (the job is slowed, nothing is down); a rack near its breaker runs its GPUs power-capped at 550 W (Warning alarm, 6 h); an inference service has held its GPUs idle for 6 h (no alarm). A driver rollout change covers the nodes on the old driver.

7.4.4User stories and acceptance criteria

US-GPU-01 As a GPU operations engineer, I want to see which jobs are stalled or slowed and why, so that I fix the costliest first (GPU-005, GPU-006, GPU-007).

  • Given a training job whose node reports Xid 79, when I open GPU Fleet → Jobs, then the job shows Stalled with the cause "GPU n on node fell off the bus (Xid 79)".
  • And the job shows the full job price per hour as lost now, and the work to redo since its last checkpoint.
  • And it is listed first when it has the highest lost-now rate.

US-GPU-02 As an operations head, I want one fleet health figure that I can trust, so that I know whether the product is healthy (GPU-009, BRULE-GPU-07/08).

  • Given 152 GPUs in a stalled job, when I view the summary, then healthy % excludes those 152 GPUs and the status is Critical.
  • Given only spares are idle, then the status is not reduced.

US-GPU-03 As a NOC engineer, I want node-level DCGM detail, so that I can confirm a hardware fault before I raise an RMA (GPU-010, GPU-012, GPU-014).

  • Given a node with a row remap pending, when I open it, then the GPU strip marks that GPU, the ECC and row remap counters are shown, and a 24 h sparkline ends on the current value.

US-GPU-04 As a facility engineer, I want to see the liquid loops feeding GPU racks, so that I catch low flow before GPUs throttle (GPU-019, GPU-020).

  • Given a CDU whose pump A has seized and flow is below 90 % of nominal, when I open Cooling & power, then the CDU shows warn / bad, lists its racks, the jobs on those racks show Slowed with the hardware-slowdown cause, and a Cooling incident links them.

US-GPU-09 As a GPU operations engineer, I want a slowed job to show even when no incident is open, so that waste is not hidden behind ticketing (GPU-005, GPU-008, BRULE-GPU-18).

  • Given an InfiniBand rail flapping on one node of a job and no incident, then the job shows Slowed with the rail as its cause, its lost-now rate, and "no incident open".
  • Given an inference allocation idle for 6 h, then it shows Idle with its lost-now rate and no alarm is raised.

US-GPU-05 As a capacity planner, I want to know where a 64-GPU H100 job fits best, so that new work goes where power is already paid (GPU-026).

  • Given I enter 64 GPUs and H100, when the list renders, then each pod shows its fit (Good / Tight / No) and the reasons, and pods with idle contracted power rank above pods that need new demand.

US-GPU-06 As a finance / ops lead, I want to see the power cost per sold GPU-hour against the price, so that I know the margin left after power (GPU-024).

  • Given a pod at 60 % utilisation, when I view power cost, then the cost per sold GPU-hour is higher than the cost per installed GPU-hour, by installed ÷ earning GPUs.

US-GPU-07 As a colocation operator, I want to see my tenants' AI cages, so that I can protect their power and cooling without touching their GPUs (GPU-021).

  • Given the colocation model, when I open GPU Fleet, then I see cages with contracted kW, draw, CDU and inlet temperature, and no jobs, nodes or DCGM fields.

US-GPU-08 As a customer success manager, I want to see which premium customers' jobs are hurt, so that I contact them before they call (GPU-005, GPU-022).

  • Given a slowed job of a premium customer, then the job row shows the premium flag and the linked incident.

7.4.7Reports and KPIs

  • Fleet health % = healthy and full-speed GPUs ÷ installed GPUs.
  • GPU utilisation in jobs = mean SM active over GPUs in a running job.
  • GPU time lost per hour = Σ lost-now per job (BRULE-GPU-03); split stalled / slowed / idle.
  • Work to redo = Σ job price per hour × hours since the last checkpoint (stalled jobs).
  • At stake = Σ job price per hour × checkpoint interval (at-risk jobs).
  • Allocation share = GPUs in a job ÷ installed GPUs; idle allocation GPUs.
  • Energy 24 h (MWh), average power as % of TDP.
  • Power cost per installed / sold GPU-hour; power share of price %.
  • Xid events per 24 h; ECC double-bit totals; throttled GPUs by reason.

7.5Datacenter alarm scope, correlation and incident enrichment

Purpose. Turn the raw alarms from every source into one normalised, de-duplicated stream that is placed on the datacenter. Decide whether each alarm counts against the site. Group related alarms into incidents enriched with impact, ranked root-cause hypotheses (with change evidence), recommended actions and verification. The alarm and the incident remain records of the existing platform modules; SynapseDC adds the datacenter intelligence. Lifecycle stage: Operate. Primary users: NOC engineer, facility engineer, incident manager, on-call engineer, operations head. Builds on: Ops Toolkit System Alerts (alarm record, routing, paging), Services & ITSM Incidents, Problems and Change Management (records, workflow, approvals), BSS Experience and Care (customer notices).

Colocation: tenant gear is never polled, so tenant-cabinet alarms come only from power (PDU, breaker, cabinet over contracted power) and environment (inlet, leak, door). A tenant over its own power budget is an alarm and a commercial matter, not a datacenter fault. Cross-connect and remote-hands tickets sit beside incidents. NeoCloud: GPU alarms (Xid, ECC, NVLink, throttle, CDU) are a domain of their own (incident type GPU). A job's service is hit by a fault on any of its nodes. Fabric alarms are reported by plane (InfiniBand, Ethernet front-end, OOB).

7.5.1Scope

In scope

  • Normalising source events into one Alert shape.
  • Alarm identity, de-duplication, clearing and flapping.
  • Severity mapping, placement on the record tree, and scope (site / edge / dependency / downstream).
  • Edge redundancy state.
  • Correlation into incidents.
  • Ranked RCA hypotheses with evidence (alarms, recent changes, topology, optics lots).
  • Impact (services, customers, premium, money).
  • Recommended action steps with owner, expected result, rollback and verify.
  • Verification checks, problem grouping, the on-call / paging chain view, and customer notice timers.

Out of scope

  • The alarm store, routing rules and notification delivery: Ops Toolkit System Alerts and Service Ops.
  • The incident, problem and change records, assignment, approval workflow and CAB: Services & ITSM.
  • Paging delivery: PagerDuty / the platform's paging.
  • Customer communication delivery: BSS Experience and Care.

7.5.2Business rules

IDRule
BRULE-ALM-01Alarm identity = source + node + resource + alarm type. A repeat updates last-seen and count; a clear closes the alarm. Sources without a clear close the alarm after the condition has been absent for two polling intervals.
BRULE-ALM-02Flapping: more than 3 raise / clear cycles in 15 minutes holds the alarm open, marked flapping (feeds flaps-24h per interface and the "rail flapping" state).
BRULE-ALM-03Alarm severities are Critical / Major / Minor / Warning, mapped from the source scale by a configuration table: syslog 0–2 / 3 / 4 / 5 (6–7 are not alarms); ServiceNow Event Management 1 / 2 / 3 / 4; Redfish Critical / Warning on a redundancy loss / Warning; UPS on battery, depleted, overload = Critical, low battery, bypass, charger = Major, fan = Minor; Xid per BRULE-GPU-10; VESDA Fire 1/2 = Critical, Action = Major, Alert = Minor.
BRULE-ALM-04Scope site = inside the datacenter (facility, racked devices, internal links). edge = a link with exactly one end in the site, counted at both ends. dependency = the far-end node as it affects the edge link, shared AAA / NTP, collectors. downstream = remote PoPs and sites that depend on this datacenter. Downstream alarms are never counted. When the datacenter is the cause, they appear as symptomOf impact under its incident.
BRULE-ALM-05Only site, edge and dependency alarms count in a datacenter's issue counts.
BRULE-ALM-06Edge redundancy per site: Isolated when no edge link is up; Redundancy lost when at least one edge link is down and at least one is up; else Redundant. Isolated makes the site Critical; Redundancy lost makes it Attention.
BRULE-ALM-07A rack-level fault (PDU breaker, cabinet power, inlet temperature) carries the rack, so every service hosted in that rack is affected. One physical fault seen on several devices carries a shared fault id and location.
BRULE-ALM-08Alarms that share a fault, a rack, a link, or a power / cooling upstream within a short window are grouped into one incident.
BRULE-ALM-09Incident severities are Critical / High / Medium / Low (stored 1–4); statuses Open, Investigating, Mitigated, Resolved, Closed; types Network, Power, Cooling, Rack, Capacity, Security, GPU. Priority P1–P4 is shown only when it differs from severity. Response SLA = 15 / 30 / 60 / 240 min and resolve SLA = 4 / 8 / 24 / 72 h for Critical / High / Medium / Low; an incident is past SLA when the open clock exceeds its target.
BRULE-ALM-10RCA hypotheses are ranked by confidence (the shares of 100 % across the listed hypotheses). Evidence = alarms, a change completed within 3 days before detection at the site or on the path, topology (an upstream device or power stage explains its downstream alarms), and the optics lot from inventory.
BRULE-ALM-11A completed change cited by an incident's leading hypothesis is marked "Caused an incident" in Change Management and counts as a failed change.
BRULE-ALM-12Incidents grouped by a common cause (for example every incident whose evidence or plan names the same optics lot, else type + normalised title) form one problem record with a workaround and a fix; its state is Fix in change (a change is open for it), Investigating (an incident is still open) or Known error.
BRULE-ALM-13Customer notices: the initial notice is due 15 / 30 / 60 min after detection for Critical / High / Medium or Low, then updates every 1 / 2 / 4 h until resolved, and a resolution notice 20 min after resolution. A missed update is flagged overdue from the minute it was due.
BRULE-ALM-14Default verification: all correlated alarms cleared; health checks green for 15 minutes; affected services back within SLA.
BRULE-ALM-15A source silent for two intervals marks its values stale and raises a monitoring-gap alarm. A device with no poll for 15 minutes becomes unmonitored. A collector silent for 15 minutes turns the site's collector chip stale.
BRULE-ALM-16An open Critical incident at a site freezes non-emergency changes there (Change Management policy).
BRULE-ALM-17On-call per region: the geography's people rotate weekly, day shift 08–20 and night shift 20–08, in the roles NOC shift lead (tier 1, escalate after 15 min), network engineer, facility engineer and field technician (tier 2, 30 / 30 / 60 min), AI infrastructure engineer at GPU sites (tier 2, 30 min) and duty manager (tier 3, 60 min, whole week). Paging chain: L1 at once; the owner's role (L2) when acknowledged or after L1's escalation time; the duty manager at 30 min for a Critical incident. Next escalation = the duty manager at 60 min while unacknowledged, else at 75 % of the resolve SLA for Critical / High. The customer's paging tool replaces the rota where one exists.
BRULE-ALM-18Similar incidents: an incident names other devices with the same signature (for example four nodes on the same optics vendor batch), which suggests a batch-level RMA rather than separate ones.

7.5.3Functional requirements

IDRequirementPriorityModelPhase
ALM-001The system shall convert every incoming event (trap, syslog, Redfish event, DCGM Xid, BACnet notification, Modbus threshold, PACS event, collector probe) into the normalised Alert shape at the collector.MBothP1
ALM-002The system shall send both the normalised alarm and the raw event to Ops Toolkit System Alerts through its API, so the alert record keeps both for audit and replay.MBothP1
ALM-003The system shall de-duplicate and clear alarms per BRULE-ALM-01.MBothP1
ALM-004The system shall detect flapping per BRULE-ALM-02 and mark the alarm flapping.MBothP1
ALM-005The system shall map source severity to Critical / Major / Minor / Warning from an editable mapping table.MBothP1
ALM-006The system shall place each alarm on site → floor → room → rack → device → port by address or serial match to the DCIM record.MBothP1
ALM-007The system shall assign each alarm a scope (site / edge / dependency / downstream) and a category (Facility / Network / Device / Performance).MBothP1
ALM-008The system shall exclude downstream alarms from every datacenter count and show them as symptoms under the causing incident.MBothP1
ALM-009The system shall compute edge redundancy per site per BRULE-ALM-06.MBothP1
ALM-010The system shall attach rack and shared-fault references per BRULE-ALM-07.MBothP1
ALM-011The system shall publish normalised alarms to Ops Toolkit System Alerts and keep the identity as the de-duplication key.MBothP1
ALM-012The system shall provide an Alerts list with an analytics strip of six tiles (active alarms by severity with the unacknowledged count, acknowledged %, correlated into incidents, categories active, raised in the last 24 h by hour, top three datacenters), filters by site, device, severity, state (open / active only / acknowledged / cleared / all), scope, category and source, and the actions Acknowledge and Correlate into incident on selected alarms.MBothP1
ALM-013The system shall correlate alarms into incidents per BRULE-ALM-08 and create or update the incident in Services & ITSM Incidents.MBothP1
ALM-014The system shall assign the incident type (Network, Power, Cooling, Rack, Capacity, Security, GPU), severity Critical / High / Medium / Low, priority, owner and reporter (Monitoring, BMS, DCIM, Capacity forecaster, Discovery), and show priority only when it differs from severity.MBothP1
ALM-015The system shall list affected objects per incident (device, link, rack, service, customer, facility).MBothP1
ALM-016The system shall compute incident impact: services, customers, premium customers, revenue at risk and a one-line summary with level High / Medium / Low.MBothP1
ALM-017The system shall produce ranked RCA hypotheses with confidence and evidence links per BRULE-ALM-10.MBothP1
ALM-018The system shall show recent changes at the site or on the affected path as evidence, and mark a change "Caused an incident" when the leading hypothesis cites it.MBothP1
ALM-019The system shall record a user's confirm / reject feedback on a hypothesis.SBothP1
ALM-020The system shall list recommended action steps with owner, expected result, rollback and verification, and track each step's status.MBothP1
ALM-021The system shall let a user raise an emergency change from an incident, linked to it.SBothP1
ALM-022The system shall run the verification checklist per BRULE-ALM-14 before an incident can be resolved.MBothP1
ALM-023The system shall show similar past incidents and the nodes they shared.CBothP2
ALM-024The system shall show the incident worklog from Services & ITSM and post its system and AI entries to it through the ITSM API (human entries are written in ITSM or through the same API).MBothP1
ALM-025The system shall group incidents into problems by common cause and show them on an Incidents → Problems tab.SBothP2
ALM-026The system shall show "On call & communications" per incident: on-call engineer from the rota or paging tool, paging chain, next escalation, ITSM reference, customer notice due times and overdue updates.SBothP2
ALM-027The system shall show the response and resolve SLA clocks per incident and flag past-SLA incidents.MBothP1
ALM-028The system shall show an incident's revenue impact panel from the Revenue Impact model.MBothP1
ALM-029The system shall raise monitoring-gap alarms through the System Alerts API and show the unmonitored device state per BRULE-ALM-15, using the freshness the OAP telemetry consumer API returns.MBothP1
ALM-030The system shall raise GPU alarms (Xid, ECC, NVLink, throttle) through the System Alerts API and open GPU-type incidents that list the affected jobs through the Services & ITSM API.MNeoP1
ALM-031The system shall raise tenant cabinet alarms from power and environment only, and treat a tenant inside its own contracted power as not a datacenter fault.MColoP1
ALM-032The system shall raise power-contract findings (block ≥ 85 % drawn, new demand peak above the floor, unexplained draw > 12 % over the 24 h mean, block ≥ 30 % idle) as Power alarms, and one Medium Power incident for the costliest block or ratchet finding.SBothP2
ALM-033The system shall show incident list views Open, Critical open, High open, Breaching SLA and All, a datacenter filter and a Problems tab; the datacenter Needs attention tab shall open with four figure tiles (past SLA, unacknowledged, oldest open, MTTR 30 d) and the list "Resolved · last 7 days".MBothP1
ALM-034The system shall emit dco.alert.* and dco.incident.* events for every lifecycle change.MBothP1
ALM-035The system shall answer Opstral AI questions about an incident's cause, what changed before it and its impact from the same RCA, change and impact data.CBothP2
ALM-036The system shall let a user correlate selected uncorrelated alarms that share a cause into one incident by hand, and show per alarm the incident it belongs to.SBothP1
ALM-037The system shall show per alarm its severity, scope, category, source, title, device and port or link, site, start time, state, count and acknowledged-by, and open the device, link or incident from the row.MBothP1
ALM-038The system shall raise hardware alarms (failed disk, failed PSU) on the operator's own servers with the RMA case they open, never marking a server unreachable for a part fault.SBothP1
ALM-039The system shall show the incident's similar incidents (BRULE-ALM-18) and the on-call rota of the site's region with the current shift.SBothP2

7.5.4User stories and acceptance criteria

US-ALM-01 As a NOC engineer, I want a single alarm for a repeating trap, so that the list is not flooded (ALM-003, ALM-004).

  • Given 40 linkDown / linkUp traps on one port in 15 minutes, when I open Alerts, then one alarm is shown, marked flapping, with its count.

US-ALM-02 As an operations head, I want a remote PoP outage caused by my backhaul not to count as a second datacenter issue, so that the counts are honest (ALM-008).

  • Given a backhaul fault at the site and an alarm at the far PoP, then the site's count includes the edge alarm only, and the PoP appears under the incident as a symptom.

US-ALM-03 As an incident manager, I want the most likely cause with its evidence, so that I act on the right fix (ALM-017, ALM-018).

  • Given a port flapping 15 h after a change re-patched its ODF core, when I open the incident, then the leading hypothesis is a physical-layer fault, the change is listed as evidence, and the change shows "Caused an incident".

US-ALM-04 As a facility engineer, I want one incident for a UPS on battery and its downstream alarms, so that I am not chasing many alarms (ALM-013).

  • Given feed A is lost and UPS-2 is on battery, then one Power incident groups the UPS, ATS and rack alarms, and its affected list includes the racks on the A side.

US-ALM-05 As an incident manager, I want the incident to sync with ServiceNow, so that my process does not change (ALM-013, ALM-024).

  • Given an incident is opened, then a ServiceNow incident is created with the correlation id, and work notes added on either side appear on both.

US-ALM-06 As an incident manager, I want to resolve only after verification, so that problems do not come back (ALM-022).

  • Given a check is still open, when I try to resolve, then the system blocks it and lists the open checks.

US-ALM-07 As a customer success manager, I want to see when the next customer update is due, so that notices are never late (ALM-026).

  • Given a Critical incident open for 80 min with no update since the initial notice at 15 min, then the update due at 75 min shows as overdue, and the next escalation names the duty manager.

US-ALM-08 As a GPU operations engineer, I want GPU faults as GPU incidents listing the hurt jobs, so that I see the business effect immediately (ALM-030).

  • Given Xid 79 on a node in a 152-GPU job, then a GPU incident lists that job as Stalled with its lost-now rate.

7.5.7Reports and KPIs

  • Open alarms by severity and scope; alarms per site (site + edge + dependency only).
  • Noise reduction = raw events ÷ normalised alarms.
  • Open incidents by severity; past SLA; unacknowledged; oldest open.
  • MTTA = mean (acknowledged − detected); MTTR 30 d = mean (resolved − detected) over incidents resolved in 30 days.
  • RCA accuracy = hypotheses confirmed ÷ (confirmed + rejected).
  • Change-caused incidents % = incidents whose leading hypothesis cites a change ÷ incidents.
  • Customer notices on time % = notices sent by their due time ÷ notices due.
  • Monitoring coverage = monitored devices ÷ installed devices.

7.6Revenue Impact

Purpose. Show what every open datacenter issue is costing now, and what it puts at stake: revenue not delivered, SLA credits triggered, the next credit cliff and a 24-hour forecast. Each figure is charged to exactly one issue, so the totals reconcile across issues, datacenters and customers. Operations can then rank work by business effect. Lifecycle stage: Operate. Primary users: operations head, incident manager, customer success / account manager, finance controller. Builds on: new (no platform module covers it). It reads BSS Customers / Orders / Catalog (contracts, charges, SLA) and ITSM Incidents.

Colocation: a tenant running hot inside its own contracted size, or over its own power budget, is not a loss (it is a commercial conversation). Losses come from the operator's faults: cabinet power loss, cooling, cross-connects and the meet-me room. Credits follow the colocation contract tiers. NeoCloud: each GPU job is a service. Domain GPU (Xid / ECC / NVLink) is a cause, and GPU heat counts as Cooling. The customer is billed the job's GPU-month rate. Revenue counts what the customer is billed; GPU Fleet separately counts productive GPU time lost.

7.6.1Scope

In scope

  • Service impact states (down, degraded, exposed) and attribution to one issue.
  • Money: lost so far, rate, credits, locked-in credits, next cliff, forecast 24 h, potential if it fails.
  • A cost-of-delay curve.
  • Views by issue, customer, datacenter and cause domain.
  • 30-day history; the "One failure away" list.
  • Revenue panels on the incident, datacenter and customer pages, and a revenue column on lists.
  • An explanation drawer ("How it is calculated").

Out of scope

  • Invoicing and credit notes: the customer's billing system (the platform has no billing module).
  • Contracts and price lists as records: BSS CRM, Customers and Plans & Products Catalog.
  • Per-contract custom credit formulas beyond the tier table (to discuss).

7.6.2Business rules

IDRule
BRULE-REV-01A service is down when its status is Down, and degraded when Degraded (both are losing now). It is exposed when it is Active but rides a Critical fault: UPS on battery, a failed cooling unit, or a Critical alarm on its own path.
BRULE-REV-02Not a loss: a service hot inside its own contracted size (utilisation causes), or a tenant over its own power budget.
BRULE-REV-03Each impacted service is charged to ONE issue = its most severe cause. Order: link / port / rack / site before device, single-PSU last. The issue is the open incident tracking that alarm; otherwise faults are grouped by site + title.
BRULE-REV-04Rate = monthly charge ÷ 730 h; × 1.0 when down, × 0.3 when degraded; 0 when exposed.
BRULE-REV-05Lost = rate × hours since the alarm began.
BRULE-REV-06SLA credit: the 30-day uptime drops by the impacted minutes (÷ 43,200 min). The shortfall below the SLA target picks the tier: > 0 → 10 %, ≥ 0.5 pt → 25 %, ≥ 2 pt → 50 % of the monthly charge. The issue is charged only the extra tier it causes.
BRULE-REV-07Credits already owed before this fault (uptime30d already below target) are "locked in". They are reported separately and booked to an earlier incident at the site, never to the current issue.
BRULE-REV-08Next cliff = the time until the next credit tier step and its amount; cliffs within 30 minutes of the first are grouped into one; the strip also counts the cliffs within 60 minutes.
BRULE-REV-09Forecast 24 h = lost + credits if the issue is still open 24 h from now. The cost-of-delay curve samples from the fault start to 24 h ahead (48 points, the past share by elapsed time); an exposed-only issue's curve starts at now and reads "if it fails now".
BRULE-REV-10Exposed: potential rate = Σ hourly charge of the exposed services. Potential credits = the tier step caused by a 4-hour outage (the Critical resolution target).
BRULE-REV-11Losses are charged to the datacenter where the fault is. A backhaul fault that degrades services homed at the far-end site is charged to the faulty site.
BRULE-REV-12Reconciliation: Σ issues = Σ causes = Σ datacenters = Σ customers = Σ site views = total.
BRULE-REV-13Issue ranking: severity weight, then rate, then potential (credits + rate × 4 h), then monthly revenue exposed.
BRULE-REV-1430-day history = resolved incidents (duration × severity weight 0.6 for Critical / High, 0.3 Medium, 0.12 Low × site average) + locked-in credits + today's open impact.
BRULE-REV-15Money is written in the geography's currency and number format. Enterprise wording is Business value / Cost of downtime / OLA rebates.
BRULE-REV-16GPU jobs: the service's monthly charge = GPUs × persona rate × model price factor. A fault on any node of the job hits that service.
BRULE-REV-17Within SLA · 30 d = the share of monthly revenue whose services meet their SLA target over 30 days. Credits owed = credits triggered by open issues + credits locked in over 30 days.
BRULE-REV-18Customer state = the worst of the customer's services: losing now (down / degraded), running on a critical fault (exposed), below SLA over 30 days, on an open incident's path, else normal; the verdict line names the main cause and opens the fault. Running hot is never a loss.

7.6.3Functional requirements

IDRequirementPriorityModelPhase
REV-001The system shall classify every service as down, degraded, exposed or not impacted per BRULE-REV-01/02.MBothP1
REV-002The system shall charge each impacted service to exactly one issue per BRULE-REV-03.MBothP1
REV-003The system shall compute rate, lost, credits, locked-in credits, next cliff, forecast 24 h, potential rate and potential credits per BRULE-REV-04 to 10.MBothP1
REV-004The system shall charge losses to the datacenter where the fault is (BRULE-REV-11).MBothP1
