7.6.4User stories and acceptance criteria

US-REV-01 As an operations head, I want to know which issue costs the most right now, so that it is fixed first (REV-007, REV-008).

  • Given two open incidents, then "Costing the most right now" names the one with the higher severity weight and rate, with its rate, lost so far and next cliff.

US-REV-02 As a finance controller, I want numbers that add up, so that I can trust them (REV-005).

  • Given any view, then Σ issues = Σ datacenters = Σ customers = total, to the unit.

US-REV-03 As an account manager, I want to know when a customer's credit steps up, so that I can escalate before it does (REV-003, REV-010).

  • Given a service at 0.4 pt below its SLA target, then its next cliff shows the minutes until 0.5 pt and the extra 15 % of the monthly charge.

US-REV-04 As an incident manager, I want to see the exposure when nothing is down yet, so that a UPS on battery gets urgency (REV-013).

  • Given UPS-2 on battery and its services still Active, then those services are exposed, the issue shows the potential rate and the potential credits after a 4-hour outage, and lost = 0.

US-REV-05 As a colocation operator, I want a tenant over its own power budget not to show as lost revenue, so that the screen is not alarmist (REV-020).

  • Given a cabinet drawing above contracted kW with no fault, then no money is charged and the cabinet appears only as a power alarm.

US-REV-06 As a NeoCloud operator, I want a stalled training job priced as a service, so that its customer impact appears (REV-019).

  • Given a stalled job of 152 GPUs, then its service is down, charged to the GPU incident, with rate = monthly charge ÷ 730.

US-REV-07 As a finance controller, I want to see how a number is computed, so that I can audit it (REV-014).

  • When I open "How it is calculated", then I see the formulas of BRULE-REV-04 to 10 with the constants 730 h, 0.3, 10 / 25 / 50 % and 4 h.

7.6.7Reports and KPIs

  • Losing now = Σ rate per hour of down + degraded services.
  • Lost so far = Σ rate × hours since the fault.
  • Credits triggered = Σ extra tier × monthly charge.
  • Forecast 24 h = Σ (lost + credits) at now + 24 h.
  • At stake = Σ potential rate × 4 h + potential credits.
  • Revenue exposed (MRR) = Σ monthly charge of impacted services.
  • Customers / premium customers affected.
  • 30-day cost of downtime by domain; locked-in credits.

7.7Datacenter Security Ops

Purpose. Give the datacenter SOC one view of security across the network, management (BMC / OOB), compute and Kubernetes, facility OT and physical access, in the datacenter context: change windows, DCIM placement, access log. This covers detections, cases, threat coverage, vulnerabilities, hardening and drift, OT zones and physical protection, mapped to industry standards. Lifecycle stage: Operate. Primary users: SOC analyst, SOC engineer, security manager / CISO, facility security, compliance officer. Builds on: Ops Toolkit Security Ops (SOC platform, case record, playbook engine). SynapseDC adds the datacenter detections, context and views.

Colocation: tenant equipment is out of scope (never monitored). Focus on the operator's network, operator servers (BMS / EPMS, access control, CCTV, DCIM, jump hosts), facility OT and physical security, including tenant cage doors. A typical story case is a jump-host compromise. NeoCloud: adds GPU-node and Kubernetes detections (privileged pods, crypto-mining on idle GPU nodes, kubectl exec), and BMC / OOB detections on GPU servers. Workload contents are never inspected.

7.7.1Scope

In scope

  • Detections from datacenter rules (Sigma-style, ATT&CK-tagged), triage, dispositions.
  • Cases following NIST SP 800-61 with playbooks.
  • MITRE ATT&CK Enterprise + ICS coverage and a rules catalogue (enable, edit, new).
  • Vulnerabilities prioritised by CVSS v4 + EPSS + CISA KEV → CISA SSVC.
  • Hardening (CIS Benchmark / DISA STIG / vendor-guide checks) and configuration drift against the last approved config.
  • OT security (IEC 62443 zones and conduits, SL-T vs SL-A).
  • Physical security (EN 50600-2-5-style protection classes, access exceptions).
  • NIST CSF 2.0 posture.
  • Security data sources and coverage gaps (Admin).

Out of scope

  • The SIEM / SOAR platform, case store and automation engine: Ops Toolkit Security Ops (or the customer's SIEM).
  • The access-control and video systems themselves (PACS / VMS).
  • Patch deployment: ITSM Change Management executes it.
  • Threat-intel subscriptions (feeds such as NVD / KEV / EPSS / PSIRT replace the illustrative advisories).

7.7.2Business rules

IDRule
BRULE-SEC-01Sites in scope = every site that is not planned.
BRULE-SEC-02Telemetry path: sensors (TACACS+ / syslog / config backups, Redfish, osquery, Falco, Kubernetes audit, Trivy, Suricata, Zeek, NetFlow, passive OT sensor, PACS, VMS) → OpenTelemetry collectors → OCSF tables → rules compiled to queries. Datacenter context (DCIM, IPAM, change windows, tickets) is joined as lookup tables.
BRULE-SEC-03A device that is not discovered sends no telemetry, and it counts as a coverage gap.
BRULE-SEC-04Physical detections ARE the access log's security exceptions (tailgating, door forced / held open, anti-passback, repeated denials, after-hours entry, unescorted visitor, overstay, badge not returned), so both screens reconcile.
BRULE-SEC-05Unauthorised configuration change (SR-002) = running config differs from the last approved backup and no approved change request covers the device in that window. These are the drift findings with no CR.
BRULE-SEC-06Example thresholds: brute force = 20 or more failed TACACS+ logins to one device from one source in 10 min. BMC burst = 10 or more failed Redfish / IPMI logins in 10 min. Management port scan = one non-monitoring source touching 50 or more management addresses / ports in 5 min. SSH spraying = one source failing on 5 or more hosts with different accounts in 15 min.
BRULE-SEC-07OT detections: a Modbus/TCP write (function 5, 6, 15 or 16) to a UPS or cooling controller from a non-BMS / EPMS host; a setpoint changed outside a maintenance change; a login with default credentials on a UPS card or PDU; a new host speaking Modbus or BACnet; BMS alarm limits raised or disabled.
BRULE-SEC-08Cross-domain rule SR-060: a badge-in at a site followed by an unapproved change on a device in that site correlates into one case.
BRULE-SEC-09SSVC decision: KEV and (exposure open or asset crown jewel) → Act. KEV → Attend. EPSS ≥ 0.1 and exposure open → Attend. EPSS ≥ 0.1, or CVSS ≥ 9 on a non-standard asset → Track*. Otherwise → Track.
BRULE-SEC-10Due dates from the advisory date: Act 7 d, Attend 30 d, Track* 90 d, Track 180 d.
BRULE-SEC-11Case phases (NIST SP 800-61): Detection & analysis → Containment → Eradication → Recovery → Post-incident.
BRULE-SEC-12OT zone achieved level (SL-A) = target level (SL-T) minus 1 for each of: an open Act / Attend firmware finding in the zone, reachability from the IT network (when SL-T > 2), an any-any conduit rule. Minimum 1.
BRULE-SEC-13OT asset risk: default credentials and reachable from IT → Critical. Default credentials, or an Act vulnerability → High. Cleartext protocol and reachable from IT → Medium. Otherwise Low.
BRULE-SEC-14Posture rolls up to the six NIST CSF 2.0 functions (Govern, Identify, Protect, Detect, Respond, Recover), each with its basis and items. Respond = 0.7 × (open cases within SLA ÷ open cases × 100) + 0.3 × max(0, 100 − MTTA in minutes); Protect = hardening pass % − min(25, 0.4 × open Act findings) − min(10, 0.3 × OT default credentials); Identify = (inventory coverage % + 100) ÷ 2; Govern = 100 − 5 × access reviews due − min(20, expired badges still active).
BRULE-SEC-15Technique ids are MITRE ATT&CK Enterprise and ICS; physical rules carry an ISO 27001 control instead (A.7.1 perimeter, A.7.2 entry, A.7.3 offices and rooms, A.5.18 access rights). Hardening check ids are SynapseDC's own, mapped to CIS / STIG sections (not CIS numbering). Advisories shown before a live feed is connected are marked illustrative.
BRULE-SEC-16The system never blocks traffic, disables accounts or changes configuration. Containment steps are done by people, through a change or the SOC's own tools.
BRULE-SEC-17Site security score = 100 − 9 × open Critical detections − 3 × High − min(14, 1.2 × Act vulnerabilities) − 6 × unauthorised drifts (7 d) − 1.5 × open physical exceptions − 0.35 × (100 − hardening pass %) − 0.3 × (100 − sensor coverage %), floored at 0.
BRULE-SEC-18Rules are Sigma rules (log source, selection, condition) compiled to a query on the security data store; each keeps its source, the compiled query, its version, status (Stable / Test / Off) and who changed it last. Wazuh is not used; the security data store is ClickHouse with OCSF tables and datacenter context as dictionaries.
BRULE-SEC-19Reference story cases the acceptance tests use, per site: SEC-0001 a badge-in at the hero site followed by an unapproved ACL change opening management access on the WAN edge router (SR-060, Critical, Containment); SEC-0002 crypto-mining on idle GPU nodes (GPU estates) or a jump-host compromise by password spraying, a new admin and cleared logs (colocation); SEC-0003 an unknown device on the OOB network probing BMCs; SEC-0004 default credentials and a Modbus write on a UPS network card; SEC-0005 the worst open physical exception. Closed cases come from true-positive detections.

7.7.3Functional requirements

IDRequirementPriorityModelPhase
SEC-001The system shall provide a Security Ops menu with sub-menus Insights, Detections, Cases, Threat coverage, Vulnerabilities, Hardening & drift, OT security and Physical security, each scoped to one datacenter or all.MBothP2
SEC-002The system shall show Insights: NIST CSF 2.0 function scores with their basis, open detections and cases, Act vulnerabilities, coverage gaps and silent sources (linking to Security data sources).MBothP2
SEC-003The system shall evaluate datacenter detection rules across the domains Network, Management, Compute, Kubernetes, Facility OT, Physical and cross-domain.MBothP2
SEC-004The system shall tag every rule and detection with ATT&CK Enterprise or ICS techniques.MBothP2
SEC-005The system shall list detections with rule, severity, asset, site, technique, state and disposition, and let an analyst triage (assign, set state, set disposition true / false / benign positive) and attach to a case.MBothP2
SEC-006The system shall raise SR-002 detections from configuration drift with no covering change (BRULE-SEC-05).MBothP2
SEC-007The system shall raise physical detections from the access log's security exceptions so the counts equal the Access tab (BRULE-SEC-04).MBothP2
SEC-008The system shall correlate the cross-domain badge-in → unapproved change rule (SR-060) into one case.SBothP2
SEC-009The system shall detect crypto-mining, privileged pods / host mounts and kubectl exec on GPU nodes.MNeoP2
SEC-010The system shall detect BMC login bursts, unknown devices on OOB ports and BMC firmware changes outside a campaign.MBothP2
SEC-011The system shall detect the OT behaviours in BRULE-SEC-07.MBothP2
SEC-012The system shall manage cases through the NIST SP 800-61 phases with step checklists, notes, closure outcome and SLA.MBothP2
SEC-013The system shall offer playbooks per case type and show each step's owner.SBothP2
SEC-014The system shall sync cases with Ops Toolkit Security Ops (or the customer's SOAR) as the case record.MBothP2
SEC-015The system shall show ATT&CK coverage (techniques with an enabled rule and a live sensor) for Enterprise and ICS.SBothP2
SEC-016The system shall provide a rules catalogue: enable / disable, edit and create a rule (title, description, domain, sensors, techniques or ISO control, level, status, playbook, Sigma source and compiled query), with version history and detections per rule over 7 days (BRULE-SEC-18).SBothP2
SEC-017The system shall match advisories to firmware, OS and packages per asset and list vulnerabilities with CVSS v4, EPSS, KEV, exposure, SSVC decision and due date (BRULE-SEC-09/10).MBothP2
SEC-018The system shall let a user set a vulnerability state (Open, In remediation with a change link, Accepted with a note, Fixed).MBothP2
SEC-019The system shall replace the illustrative advisories with live NVD / CISA KEV / FIRST EPSS / vendor PSIRT feeds when they are connected.SBothP3
SEC-020The system shall run hardening checks per baseline (for example CIS Cisco IOS XR, DISA STIG NX-OS, CIS Junos, vendor guides for BMCs, UPS cards and PDUs) per asset and show pass / fail with remediation and the pass % per baseline and site.SBothP2
SEC-021The system shall show configuration drift per device (running vs last approved) as authorised (with a change) or unauthorised.MBothP2
SEC-022The system shall show IEC 62443 zones and conduits per site with SL-T, SL-A (BRULE-SEC-12), assets and findings.SBothP2
SEC-023The system shall list OT assets (UPS cards, PDUs, BMS / CDU controllers, meters, DG controllers) with default credentials, cleartext, IT reachability and risk (BRULE-SEC-13).MBothP2
SEC-024The system shall show physical protection layers per site (perimeter → building → room → cage / rack) with EN 50600-2-5-style classes, controls and exceptions.SBothP2
SEC-025The system shall show Security data sources (in Admin) with each sensor's state, last event, assets covered and gaps.MBothP2
SEC-026The system shall send detections, cases and the audit trail to the SIEM as OCSF.MBothP2
SEC-027The system shall never perform containment itself (BRULE-SEC-16), and offer "raise change" for remediation.MBothP2
SEC-028The system shall exclude tenant-owned equipment from assets, detections and vulnerabilities.MColoP2
SEC-029The system shall show metrics: MTTA, MTTR, open cases within SLA, true-positive rate.SBothP2
SEC-030The system shall map security posture to ISO 27001 controls for Compliance (A.5–A.8, A.7 physical).CBothP3
SEC-031The system shall show a site security score per datacenter (BRULE-SEC-17) and rank sites by it on Insights.SBothP2
SEC-032The system shall keep every triage, case, vulnerability and rule move as the analyst's work in the Security Ops record so it survives a data refresh, and audit who made it.MBothP2
SEC-033The system shall list, per detection, the datacenter context it was raised with: site, room, rack, owner or tenant, the change request covering the asset, and who was badged into the room.MBothP2
SEC-034The system shall show per playbook its trigger rules and steps by phase with the system each step runs on and whether it is automatic; automatic steps run in the SOC platform, never in SynapseDC (BRULE-SEC-16).SBothP2

7.7.4User stories and acceptance criteria

US-SEC-01 As a SOC analyst, I want an unapproved config change flagged with the person who badged in, so that I can investigate insider activity (SEC-006, SEC-008).

  • Given a badge-in at the site and a WAN-edge ACL change with no approved CR, then one case links the access event, the drift and the device, with phase Detection & analysis.

US-SEC-02 As a SOC analyst, I want to see crypto-mining on idle GPU nodes, so that stolen GPU time is stopped (SEC-009).

  • Given a mining-pool connection from a GPU node in an idle allocation, then a High detection on rule SR-032 is shown with its ATT&CK technique, linked to the node and its job.

US-SEC-03 As a vulnerability manager, I want an SSVC decision per finding, so that I patch what matters first (SEC-017).

  • Given a KEV advisory on an internet-exposed firewall, then the decision is Act with a due date 7 days from the advisory.
  • Given EPSS 0.03 and no KEV on a standard asset, then the decision is Track.

US-SEC-04 As a facility security officer, I want OT risks on power gear, so that default credentials on a UPS card are fixed (SEC-023, SEC-011).

  • Given a UPS card with default credentials reachable from IT, then its risk is Critical, and a Modbus write to it from a non-BMS host raises a detection.

US-SEC-05 As a security manager, I want the physical exceptions in the SOC, so that one team sees all (SEC-007).

  • Given 3 tailgating exceptions in the access log today at a site, then Physical security shows 3 detections for that site.

US-SEC-06 As a CISO, I want posture by NIST CSF function, so that I can report it (SEC-002).

  • When I open Insights, then six function scores show, each with its basis and items.

US-SEC-07 As a SOC engineer, I want to see which devices send no telemetry, so that gaps are closed (SEC-025).

  • Given devices not discovered, then they are listed as coverage gaps under the sensor that should cover them.

7.7.7Reports and KPIs

  • Open detections by severity and domain; true-positive rate = TP ÷ triaged.
  • Open cases by phase; within SLA %; MTTA, MTTR.
  • ATT&CK coverage % = techniques with an enabled rule and live sensor ÷ techniques in scope.
  • Vulnerabilities by SSVC; Act open; overdue vs due date.
  • Hardening pass % per baseline; unauthorised drifts open.
  • OT zones below target (SL-A < SL-T); OT assets Critical / High.
  • Physical exceptions per site per day.
  • NIST CSF 2.0 function scores.

7.8Capacity and power economics

Purpose. This module shows how much space, power, cooling, ports and GPU capacity each datacenter has: what is used, what is held against it, and what is truly free. It also shows what power costs under the operator's tariff and power contract. It answers three questions. Can we take the next customer or job? Where do we run out first? How much are we paying for power we do not use? Lifecycle stage: Plan / Operate. Primary users: capacity planner, facilities manager, commercial / sales engineer, finance (energy cost), GPU platform owner. Builds on: Plan to Build Inventory Planning (capacity plans and records), Plan to Build Inventory (racks, devices), BSS Customers (contracted kW). Colocation: capacity is sold as space and contracted kW per cabinet. Contracted kW above the cabinet's draw counts as a committed hold. Tenant power density and power over contract are the key figures. NeoCloud: capacity is sold as GPUs. The GPU view splits the fleet into in a job, allocated to an idle job, free and out of service, per GPU model. Power economics gives the power cost per installed and per sold GPU-hour, and shows where the next job can run on power that is already paid for.

7.8.1Scope

  • In:
  • capacity per datacenter, hall and dimension (space U, power kW, cooling kW, ports, GPUs);
  • reservations and holds from every source;
  • net available capacity;
  • forecast against the 85 % warning line, with approved and executing plans netted in;
  • network bandwidth and port headroom (links in countdown);
  • the financial view: CapEx approved and committed, OpEx per month, per site;
  • PUE, energy per day and CO₂;
  • power tariffs and power contracts per site, and the monthly power bill (energy and capacity charges);
  • idle committed kW and its cost, and cost per sold MW-month;
  • GPU power cost per GPU-hour, per job and per tenant;
  • power findings against the contracts, which raise alarms and incidents;
  • GPU job placement ranking (where to place the next job).
  • Out:
  • capacity plan records and approvals (Plan to Build Inventory Planning, through the Planner, see 7.9);
  • customer contracts and their contracted kW (BSS Customers / CRM, read);
  • billing of power to tenants (customer billing system; we only send metered usage, NB-07);
  • renewables share and WUE (not modelled, not shown).

7.8.2Business rules

IDRule
BRULE-CAP-01Net available = installed capacity − used − holds. Holds are Reserved, Committed, Pipeline or Requested, and are counted per dimension.
BRULE-CAP-02Hold sources and states:<br>• planned device = its U plus its device type's typical draw (Reserved);<br>• colocation contracted kW above the cabinet draw (Committed);<br>• approved or executing capacity plan (Pipeline);<br>• submitted plan (Requested);<br>• Planner hold (Reserved, or Committed once the plan is executing);<br>• GPU nodes allocated to an idle job (Reserved).
BRULE-CAP-03PUE = facility power ÷ IT power, from the site power split. Energy per day = facility kW × 24 h.
BRULE-CAP-04CO₂ = energy × the national grid factor: India CEA v19 = 0.716 kg/kWh; USA eGRID 2022 = 0.371 kg/kWh. The edition used is shown on screen.
BRULE-CAP-05Power bill per month = energy charge + capacity charge.<br>• Energy charge = facility load × 24 h × 30.4 days × tariff.<br>• Utility capacity charge = demand charge × max(peak kW, take-or-pay floor).<br>• Colocation capacity charge = the whole committed block × the rate per committed kW-month.
BRULE-CAP-06Idle kW = billed kW − peak kW. Idle cost per month = idle kW × the demand or committed-kW rate.
BRULE-CAP-07Sold kW = the power of GPU nodes in a running (not idle) job + tenant contracted power. Cost per sold MW-month = bill ÷ sold MW. When nothing is sold, the cost per IT MW is shown instead.
BRULE-CAP-08Power per installed GPU-hour = kW per GPU × site PUE × tariff. Power per sold GPU-hour = the same power spread over earning GPUs only. Both are compared with the GPU model's price (share %, and what is left after power).
BRULE-CAP-09Job or tenant power per day = node kW × PUE × tariff × 24, compared with what the job earns. An idle allocation is billed and drawing power but doing no work.
BRULE-CAP-10Finding "block near limit" (colocation block): peak ≥ 85 % of the committed block. Severity Major at ≥ 90 %, otherwise Minor. Exposure = the next 100 kW tranche for a year (100 kW × rate per committed kW-month × 12); power above the block bills at 1.5× the block rate.
BRULE-CAP-11Finding "demand ratchet" (utility contract): a new 12-month demand peak above the take-or-pay floor. Prior peak = the highest of the first eleven months of history × 1.06. Exposure = (new peak − prior peak) × demand rate × 12. Severity Major when the exposure exceeds half a monthly bill, otherwise Minor.
BRULE-CAP-12Finding "unexplained rise": current draw more than 12 % above the 24 h mean, with no change in progress at the site and no job started in the last 24 h. Severity Minor. Exposure = the rise × tariff × 24 h × 30.4 days per month if it holds.
BRULE-CAP-13Finding "idle block" (colocation block): idle kW ≥ 30 % of the committed block. Severity Warning. Exposure = idle cost per month × 12.
BRULE-CAP-14Each finding raises one alarm, code PWR-<site>-<KIND> (source Power, category Facility, scope site). The costliest block-near-limit or demand-ratchet finding also opens one Medium Power incident (reported by the EPMS meter check) whose impact is the money exposure and no services; its hypotheses are recent GPU job starts, cooling overhead and meter error; its actions are to cap inference pods in the peak window, place new jobs where power is already paid, and get the supplier's confirmation or a block uplift.
BRULE-CAP-15Placement of a GPU request (8–256 GPUs of a chosen model) is ranked per pod. Fit = no when the pod has no free healthy node of the model or its cooling loop (CDU for liquid pods) is in fault; tight when cooling is degraded in the room, a rack is at ≥ 90 % of budget, or the contracted headroom is below the request's load; else good. Each pod also shows free healthy GPUs, rack headroom, cooling headroom, already-paid kW (idle block, or draw below the take-or-pay floor) and marginal power per GPU-hour (kW per busy GPU × PUE × tariff). Rank by fit, then by marginal cost.
BRULE-CAP-16Tariffs and contract values are marked illustrative until the customer's tariff sheets and contracts are loaded. Local prices are never currency-converted.
BRULE-CAP-17Capacity forecast per dimension = compound growth on today's use over 12 months, with approved and executing plans landing about 3 months out; 12 months of history (metered for power, back-computed from the trend for the rest). The warning line is 85 %; a dimension within 9 months of it is flagged. Links: compound growth of the last six monthly peaks, flagged when 85 % is crossed within 6 months.
BRULE-CAP-18Illustrative contract seeding until replaced: a colocation block = peak × (1.10–1.45) rounded up to 100 kW, never above site capacity, floor 100 %, escalator 2.5–4 %; a utility connection = design capacity × (1.00–1.15), floor 60–85 %, escalator 2–4.5 %; demand charge $14 per kW-month (₹420 per kVA-month); block rate $145 (₹7,800) per committed kW-month.
BRULE-CAP-19The pitch-deck reference line (1.2 kW per GPU at PUE 1.46 at the blended tariff) is shown beside the measured cost per GPU-hour.

7.8.4User stories and acceptance criteria

US-CAP-01 As a capacity planner, I want net available power per hall after every hold, so that I don't sell capacity that is already promised. (CAP-003, CAP-004, CAP-005)

  • Given a hall with planned devices, tenant contracts and an approved plan, when I open Capacity → Space, then available = installed − used − Σ holds, and each hold links to its source.

US-CAP-02 As a colocation sales engineer, I want to see contracted kW against drawn kW per cabinet, so that I know which tenants can grow and where the operator is over-committed. (CAP-006)

  • Given a cabinet contracted at 10 kW and drawing 6 kW, when I open Reservations, then a 4 kW Committed hold is listed for that tenant.

US-CAP-03 As a finance analyst, I want the monthly power bill per site split into energy and capacity charges, so that I can check the supplier invoice. (CAP-010, CAP-012)

  • Given a site on a colocation block, when I open Power economics, then the capacity charge = committed kW × rate, whatever the draw.

US-CAP-04 As a commercial lead, I want to see idle committed power ranked by cost, so that I can resize or resell the block. (CAP-013)

  • Given a site where 30 % or more of the block is never drawn, then an "Idle committed power" finding is listed, with yearly exposure = idle cost × 12.

US-CAP-05 As a GPU platform owner, I want power cost per sold GPU-hour per pod, so that I know my margin after power. (CAP-016)

  • Given a pod with idle GPUs, then the cost per sold GPU-hour is higher than the cost per installed GPU-hour, and both are shown against the model price.

US-CAP-06 As a NOC engineer, I want an alarm when a power block nears its limit, so that the overage is avoided. (CAP-019, CAP-020)

  • Given a block drawn at 91 %, then a Major alarm PWR-<site>-BLOCKNEARLIMIT is active, and Power economics links to it and to the Medium incident it opened.

US-CAP-07 As a GPU scheduler owner, I want a ranked list of pods for 64 H100 GPUs, so that the job lands where power is already paid for and cooling has headroom. (CAP-023)

  • Given the request, when I open Where to place, then the pods are ranked by fit (good, tight, no) and then by marginal cost per GPU-hour, and a pod with a CDU fault is marked no fit.

US-CAP-08 As a sustainability lead, I want PUE and CO₂ per site with the grid factor named, so that I can report them. (CAP-008)

  • The factor's source and edition are shown next to the CO₂ figure.

7.9Planner

Purpose. The Planner turns a capacity need into a designed, costed, scheduled and approved plan:

  • a rack, pod or hall fit-out;
  • a plant upgrade or a network extension;
  • a new datacenter or an expansion;
  • a refresh or a migration.

It sizes the plan from templates and benchmarks, checks it against rules, compares scenarios and routes it for approval by CapEx band. It then hands the approved plan to Programs & Projects for delivery. Lifecycle stage: Plan → Build handoff. Primary users: capacity planner, design authority, finance manager, facilities director, executive approver, programme manager. Builds on:

  • Plan to Build Inventory Planning (plan record and holds);
  • Plan to Build Inventory and IP Management (site baseline, addresses);
  • Supply Chain Materials, Vendors, Contracts and Warehouses (catalogue, stock, vendor contracts);
  • Programs & Projects (delivery record).

Colocation: typical plans are a cage or hall fit-out for a tenant, a power upgrade, a new hall, or rack replacement. Sizing is in contracted kW per cabinet. NeoCloud: typical plans are a GPU pod or hall (liquid cooling and CDUs, InfiniBand fabric), a plant upgrade for high-density racks, a server refresh, or a new site. Sizing is in GPUs, kW per rack and cooling type.

7.9.1Scope

  • In:
  • plan families and their plan types:
  • in-site: rack, pod, hall, retrofit;
  • plant;
  • network;
  • build: new datacenter, expansion, portfolio, edge;
  • replace: server refresh, network refresh, rack replacement;
  • migration;
  • templates with inheritance, versions and variants;
  • benchmarks (costs, efficiency, lead times, sizing and staffing, rules);
  • the wizard;
  • the planner engine: sizing, CapEx / OpEx, NPV / IRR / payback, schedule from lead times, rule checks, readiness;
  • scenarios;
  • the approval path by CapEx band;
  • bill of materials and sourcing;
  • the plan pack (drawings, IP plan, BoM, CSV);
  • the review pack (PDF, XLSX, ZIP) and the review portal;
  • the edit policy and revisions;
  • the baseline;
  • the handoff to delivery.
  • Out:
  • delivery execution (work orders, purchase orders, commissioning, handover): owned by Programs & Projects, shown through the analytics pages (8.1 to 8.3);
  • purchase-order issue: Supply Chain Procurement;
  • IP allocation of record: Plan to Build IP Management;
  • the asset record after build: Plan to Build Inventory.

7.9.2Business rules

IDRule
BRULE-PLN-01A template inherits from its parent (resolved chain). A plan's effective input = plan override → template default → benchmark → planner-derived value, and the source is shown for each input.
BRULE-PLN-02A content change to a template bumps its version. Plans keep the version they were made from, and template drift is flagged on the plan.
BRULE-PLN-03CapEx band (delegation of authority):<br>• ≤ $50k Operational spend → Capacity Planner;<br>• ≤ $250k Departmental budget → Finance Manager;<br>• ≤ $1M Site programme → Finance Manager, then Facilities Director, with a business case;<br>• above $1M Capital programme → Finance Manager, then Executive, with a business case.
BRULE-PLN-04The approval path = the template's path with the band's roles inserted at the finance step, or before the first band role, or at the end. The requester and the submitter cannot approve their own plan.
BRULE-PLN-05Hard rule failures block submission. Soft rules warn. An approved demo or seeded plan that fails a hard rule on the live estate goes back to Draft, with the reason.
BRULE-PLN-06Schedule = phases from lead-time benchmarks. An item is late when its order-by date has passed. An approved plan that has not started is scheduled no earlier than today.
BRULE-PLN-07Figures follow the scenario in order: approved → in review → preferred → base.
BRULE-PLN-08The bill of materials is priced once from the catalogue. Sourcing order per line: spare site capacity, then unheld stock, then the catalogue vendor under its contract. Edits to the bill add one CapEx line, "Bill of materials changes".
BRULE-PLN-09Edit policy, the same for every editor on the plan page:<br>• Draft / Rejected = edit in place;<br>• In review = "Withdraw and save" (the round stays in History);<br>• Approved / Executing = "Draft revision B" (the edit is applied to the revision, audited "Raised to change: …"; map fields are re-based so the revision keeps its approved overrides); an open draft revision takes further edits ("Save to revision B"); a revision in review makes the plan read-only until it is decided;<br>• On hold / Completed / Cancelled / superseded = read-only, with the reason.<br>Risks and permits are a live register: status, rating, owner and mitigation save straight to any plan; adding, removing or renaming items drafts a revision.
BRULE-PLN-10On approval a baseline is frozen: CapEx, contingency, OpEx, schedule, scenario and template version. Capacity and rack-unit holds are written.
BRULE-PLN-11"Start the build" hands the plan to Programs & Projects with the sourced BoM, vendor orders (one per vendor and phase), stock and rack holds, IP and the plan type's records. Handover in delivery completes the plan, and completing the plan signs the handover.
BRULE-PLN-12Money is held in USD in the engine and shown in the geography's currency (India rate 50).
BRULE-PLN-13Reviewers for "Send for review" = the holders of the approval path's roles minus the requester and the submitter. The request records who answered; the hub shows "Your review" to a reviewer who has not answered and "Review sent" to the planner. No e-mail is sent by the prototype (to discuss).
BRULE-PLN-14A plan's tab bar always fits one row: tabs that do not fit fold into "More". Family tabs use short labels (Equipment, Inventory, Waves, Plant, Paths, Site fit).
BRULE-PLN-15Record changes at closeout (wave close, disposal certificates, the migration meter reading, site retirement) go through one audited action, allowed on executing and completed plans only.

7.9.4User stories and acceptance criteria

US-PLN-01 As a capacity planner, I want to create a GPU pod plan from a template, so that sizing, cost and schedule come from agreed benchmarks. (PLN-005, PLN-006, PLN-033)

  • Given the "GPU pod" type, when I enter 512 GPUs at a liquid-cooled site, then the wizard fills the derived inputs with their sources, and the live estimate shows CapEx and IT kW.

US-PLN-02 As a finance manager, I want plans routed to me by CapEx band, so that delegation of authority is respected. (PLN-014)

  • Given a plan with $600k CapEx, when it is submitted, then the path includes Finance Manager, then Facilities Director, and a business case is required.

US-PLN-03 As an approver, I want to review the plan in a portal and comment per section, so that the questions are traceable. (PLN-021)

  • When I comment on section 4.1 and the planner replies and resolves it, then the thread shows resolved, and the hub no longer lists it for me.

US-PLN-04 As a planner, I want to edit an approved plan without losing the approval, so that the change is reviewed as revision B. (PLN-023)

  • Given an Approved plan, when I change the phases, then the save button reads "Draft revision B", and History records "Raised to change: …".

US-PLN-05 As a programme manager, I want an approved plan to arrive in Programs & Projects with orders and holds, so that delivery starts from the same numbers. (PLN-026)

  • When "Start the build" runs, then the delivery POs equal the plan's vendor orders, and Capacity shows the planner holds.

US-PLN-06 As a design authority, I want blocking rules shown with fix links, so that a plan cannot be submitted while it fails them. (PLN-012)

  • Given a failing hard rule, then Submit is disabled and the blocker links to its step.

US-PLN-07 As a reviewer, I want a PDF / XLSX / ZIP review pack, so that I can review offline. (PLN-020)

  • The ZIP manifest checksums match its files, and the PDF has no blank pages.

7.10DCIM — racks, power chain and cabling

Purpose. DCIM holds the physical model that monitoring, impact and planning depend on:

  • where every rack and device sits;
  • how power reaches each device (UPS → busway or panel → PDU → outlet → PSU);
  • how every cable runs (port → patch panel → trunk → ODF → cross-connect).

These datacenter-specific records extend the Plan to Build Inventory asset record. They are changed only under a change request. Lifecycle stage: Build / Operate. Primary users: datacenter engineer, field technician, capacity planner, NOC (trace), colocation operations. Builds on: Plan to Build Inventory (asset record: site, device, serial, model, lifecycle), Services & ITSM Change Management (change requests). Colocation: tenant cabinets hold opaque "Tenant gear" (owner, tenant, contracted kW; never polled). The floor plan's Tenant colouring compares draw with contracted kW. Cross-connects and meet-me-room ODFs are central. NeoCloud: GPU nodes with 4 or more PSUs. High-density liquid racks with CDU manifolds. InfiniBand rails (two leafs per node) and front-end Ethernet cabling.

7.10.1Scope

  • In (datacenter-specific extensions to the Inventory record):
  • hall kind;
  • rows (paired face to face around a cold aisle);
  • rack grid position, rack budget kW, rack type and condition;
  • device U position and height;
  • components (ports, power ports with their outlet, management, console, module bays);
  • inventory items such as optics;
  • power panels (a hall's A / B busway or −48 V distribution) and feeds (rack PDUs);
  • PSU cords to PDU outlets;
  • data cables with their structured route;
  • device and module types with component templates;
  • rack-unit reservations;
  • floor plan colourings;
  • elevation drag-to-move;
  • the Explorer;
  • DCIM change control.
  • Out:
  • the asset master record: serial, model, purchase, warranty and lifecycle state (Plan to Build Inventory);
  • change approval workflow (Services & ITSM Change Management);
  • IP address records (Plan to Build IP Management; the DCIM device drawer only reads and assigns through it);
  • purchase of devices (Supply Chain).

7.10.2Business rules

IDRule
BRULE-DCIM-01Hierarchy: site → floor → hall (a room; its kind comes from its name: meet-me room, data hall…) → row → rack → device → components. Racks per row = max(2, min(8, ⌈√(n × 2.2)⌉)) for n racks in the hall. Rows are paired face to face around a cold aisle.
BRULE-DCIM-02Device status is one of: Active, Planned, Maintenance, Decommissioning, Tenant-owned. A Planned device reserves its U range and its type's typical power.
BRULE-DCIM-03U occupancy is checked on install and move: no clash with devices, the ODF block or reservations, and the device must fit within the rack height.
BRULE-DCIM-04Power: each rack PDU side (A / B) is rated at the rack budget. Σ PDU load = rack draw, and Σ feeds on a panel = panel load. Odd PSUs go to A and even PSUs to B. A new feed's rating = V × A × 0.8 (× √3 when three-phase), the 80 % continuous rating. Panel A of a hall is fed by one UPS module (UPS-2 / 4) and panel B by another (UPS-1 / 3).
BRULE-DCIM-05A single point of failure is flagged when a device has both cords on one side, a PSU has failed, a PDU is tripped, or a cabinet is at ≥ 90 % of budget (one side cannot carry it).
BRULE-DCIM-06Cabling (TIA-942): port → optic → patch cord → cabinet panel → trunk (2 tray routes; Tier II = 1) → ODF → cross-connect → far end. In-rack = one cord. Backhaul adds the outside-plant core. Cables = every LLDP link + every power cord.
BRULE-DCIM-07Connect: only a free port to a free port of the same speed at the same site. Disconnect frees both ends.
BRULE-DCIM-08Every DCIM edit except the catalogue runs under a change request. There are three options:<br>• Standard (pre-approved record work: patch cords, space holds, port label / enable, hot-swap PSU or fan) is raised and closed at once as a Standard change on template STD-07 "DCIM record:" (STD-02 / STD-05 for cords and hot swaps). It is refused at a site under a freeze that allows emergency changes only (an open Critical incident).<br>• Open CR at the site: Approved / In progress = applied now; Draft / Pending = saved as planned work.<br>• Raise new: a change request in Pending approval, origin DCIM.
BRULE-DCIM-09Planned work runs once its change is approved and not blocked by policy. A DCIM-raised change completes when all its work is done. Records are tagged with the change id (rack, device, interface, module, reservation, panel, feed), and audit lines carry it.
BRULE-DCIM-10A rack can be retired only when it is empty. A reserved U range is respected by install and move until it is released.
BRULE-DCIM-11A planned device that was recorded before DCIM change control carries the open delivery change request of its rack. Records touched by a change are tagged (rack, device, port, module slot, reservation, panel, feed) and every audit line names the change.
BRULE-DCIM-12Invariants checked on every data change: racks and devices equal the estate; Σ hall = site; rows partition a hall's racks; a rack's U = Σ devices + the ODF block, with no U clash; Σ feeds = rack draw; Σ panel = Σ feeds; cables = LLDP links + power cords; Σ devices per type = devices.

7.10.3Functional requirements

IDRequirementPriorityModelPhase
DCIM-001The system shall present the hierarchy site → floor → hall → row → rack → device → components, reading the assets from the Plan to Build Inventory API by their asset ids and storing only the datacenter-specific extensions the Inventory API cannot carry (ARCH-012, OD-01).MBothP1

DCIM-042The system shall reject a DCIM write that fails validation (U clash, budget, reserved range, freeze, missing change request) with a message naming the reason, and leave the record unchanged.MBothP1

7.10.4User stories and acceptance criteria

US-DCIM-01 As a datacenter engineer, I want to see which UPS, busway, PDU and outlet feed a server, so that I know what a power fault hits. (DCIM-005, DCIM-008)

  • Given a GPU node, when I open its power, then each PSU cord shows outlet → PDU side → busway → UPS module, and Σ PDU load = rack draw.

US-DCIM-02 As a NOC engineer, I want a cable traced hop by hop, so that the field team goes to the right patch panel. (DCIM-009)

  • Given a link, then the path lists port, optic, cord, panel, trunk, ODF, cross-connect and the far end.

US-DCIM-03 As a field technician, I want to drag a device to free units, so that a move is quick and checked. (DCIM-013, DCIM-020)

  • When I drop it on a clashing range, then the preview is red and nothing moves. When I drop it on free units, then the Move dialog asks for a change request.

US-DCIM-04 As a change manager, I want every DCIM edit tied to a change request, so that the record never changes without approval. (DCIM-020, DCIM-021)

  • Given "raise new", then a change request in Pending approval with origin DCIM exists and the edit is planned work. After approval, Execute applies it and the change request completes.

US-DCIM-05 As a colocation operations lead, I want the floor plan coloured by tenant draw vs contracted kW, so that I see who is near their limit. (DCIM-007, DCIM-028)

  • A cabinet at ≥ 90 % of contract is coloured as a warning.

US-DCIM-06 As a planner, I want to reserve 4U in a rack for an approved plan, so that nobody installs there. (DCIM-016)

  • When an install targets the reserved range, then it is refused, with the reservation named.

US-DCIM-07 As an engineer, I want a Standard change refused during an incident freeze, so that record work does not interfere with a live incident. (DCIM-020)

  • Given an open Critical incident at the site, then the Standard option is refused, with the freeze named.

7.10.6Integrations

7.11Compliance

Purpose. This module checks the estate continuously against operating policies, and maps the policy results to the controls of the standards the operator is audited on. Evidence comes from live data, not spreadsheets. Manual controls carry an attestation date. Lifecycle stage: Operate. Primary users: compliance / risk officer, facilities manager, auditor (read-only), security lead. Builds on: Ops Toolkit Security Ops (security posture evidence), Services & ITSM Change Management (change policy evidence). Colocation: ISO 27001 A.7 physical controls, ISO 22237 and TIA-942 resilience evidence, and SOC 2 evidence shared with tenants (export). NeoCloud: the same policies, plus GPU-density power and cooling evidence (CP-01, CP-03). SOC 2 for the GPU service.

7.11.1Scope

  • In:
  • policies CP-01 to CP-09 evaluated on the estate;
  • frameworks with control mappings;
  • policy-as-code (Rego source per policy, switch on / off);
  • decision records (deny / warn / allow, with input);
  • violation outcome Blocked or Warned, with a change link;
  • attestations for manual controls;
  • new policy, control and framework;
  • a posture dashboard.
  • Out:
  • audit management and auditor workflow (to discuss);
  • security findings and vulnerabilities (Ops Toolkit Security Ops, read as evidence);
  • change approval (Change Management).

7.11.2Business rules

IDRule
BRULE-CMP-01CP-01: a live rack draws ≤ 80 % of its budget. Warn.
BRULE-CMP-02CP-02: every powered device draws from both A and B. Block.
BRULE-CMP-03CP-03: every floor keeps N+1: cooling kW − the largest unit's kW ≥ heat kW. Block.
BRULE-CMP-04CP-04: every UPS holds its load ≥ 10 min on battery. Warn.
BRULE-CMP-05CP-05: management addresses are inside the site plan and unique in their VRF. Warn.
BRULE-CMP-06CP-06: no installed device is past vendor end of support. Warn.
BRULE-CMP-07CP-07: no open change breaks a blocking change policy (rollback plan, freeze). Block.
BRULE-CMP-08CP-08: every fire zone has its quarterly inspection done or booked. Warn.
BRULE-CMP-09CP-09: no plant preventive maintenance is overdue. Warn.
BRULE-CMP-10An automated control's status follows the policies that evidence it: no active policy (switched off, or only user-added policies) = Not assessed; no finding = Compliant; a finding on a blocking policy, or findings on more than 5 % of the evaluated resources = Non-compliant; otherwise Partial. A manual control is Compliant while attested within a year, else Evidence due; a user-added manual control is Evidence due until first attested.
BRULE-CMP-12Frameworks and controls: ISO/IEC 27001:2022 (A.7.11 supporting utilities ← CP-02, CP-04; A.7.13 equipment maintenance ← CP-09; A.8.32 change management ← CP-07; A.8.9 configuration management ← CP-05; A.7.2 physical entry and A.5.30 ICT readiness, manual), ISO/IEC 22237 (22237-3 power ← CP-01, CP-02, CP-04; 22237-4 environmental ← CP-03; 22237-6 security systems ← CP-08; 22237-7 management information ← CP-09, CP-06), TIA-942-C (Electrical ← CP-02, CP-01; Mechanical ← CP-03; Telecom structured cabling records, manual), SOC 2 (CC8.1 ← CP-07; A1.2 ← CP-03, CP-04, CP-08; CC7.1 ← CP-06, CP-05; CC6.4 physical access, manual). A control is evidenced only by policies that actually test it.
BRULE-CMP-11Each finding is a decision record (deny or warn). Passing resources give one allow per policy and site, with the evaluated input kept.

7.11.3Functional requirements

IDRequirementPriorityModelPhase
CMP-001The system shall evaluate policies CP-01 to CP-09 on every data cycle (BRULE-CMP-01 to BRULE-CMP-09).MBothP3
CMP-002The system shall show each policy with its resources checked, violations, outcome (Blocked / Warned) and a link to the resource.MBothP3
CMP-003The system shall map controls of ISO/IEC 27001:2022, ISO/IEC 22237, TIA-942 and SOC 2 to the policies that evidence them.MBothP3
CMP-004The system shall compute each control's status (BRULE-CMP-10).MBothP3
CMP-005The system shall hold each policy as code (Rego package and source), viewable by the compliance officer.SBothP3
CMP-006The system shall let an authorised user switch a policy on or off. An off policy stops counting, and controls it alone evidences become Not assessed.MBothP3
CMP-007The system shall keep decision records with the OPA-style input (BRULE-CMP-11).SBothP3
CMP-008The system shall let a user attest a manual control with a date, owner and evidence note, and flag attestations that are due.MBothP3
CMP-009The system shall let a user create a policy, control or framework.SBothP3
CMP-010The system shall show a posture dashboard: overall ring, status tiles, framework cards, controls that need work, policy results, evidence due, latest decisions.MBothP3
CMP-011The system shall link a CP-07 violation to its change and block the change from starting.MBothP3
CMP-012The system shall scope compliance to one datacenter or the estate.MBothP3
CMP-013The system shall export an evidence pack per framework (CSV / PDF) for auditors and tenants (to discuss).SBothP3
CMP-014The system shall read security posture findings from Ops Toolkit Security Ops as control evidence.SBothP3
CMP-015The system shall send compliance decisions to the SIEM feed (NB-10).CBothP3
CMP-016The system shall show colocation tenant-facing controls (physical access, power resilience) as a shareable view (to discuss).CColoP3
CMP-017The system shall record every policy, control and attestation change in the audit trail.MBothP3
CMP-018The system shall list every violation (policy, resource, site, message, outcome, raised time, change link) with search and a policy filter, and show the audit of compliance changes.MBothP3
CMP-019The system shall evidence each control only by the policies that test it (BRULE-CMP-12) and show the evidence text (resources evaluated, findings, or the attestation).MBothP3

7.11.4User stories and acceptance criteria

US-CMP-01 As a compliance officer, I want ISO 27001 A.7.11 status from live power data, so that the evidence is current. (CMP-003, CMP-004)

  • Given a device on one PDU side, then CP-02 shows Blocked, and A.7.11 shows Non-compliant with that device listed.

US-CMP-02 As a compliance officer, I want to switch off a policy that does not apply, so that it stops distorting posture. (CMP-006)

  • When CP-05 is off, then its violations stop counting, and controls evidenced only by it show Not assessed.

US-CMP-03 As a facilities manager, I want overdue fire inspections flagged, so that I book them. (CMP-001)

  • Given a zone with an overdue inspection and nothing booked, then CP-08 lists it with a link to Facilities → Environment.

US-CMP-04 As an auditor, I want the decision record behind a finding, so that I can see what was evaluated. (CMP-007)

  • When I open a finding, then I see the policy, the resource, the outcome and the input values.

US-CMP-05 As a risk officer, I want manual controls attested with a date, so that the due ones are visible. (CMP-008)

  • Given an attestation older than one year, then the control shows Evidence due.

US-CMP-06 As a change manager, I want a change that breaks a blocking policy flagged, so that it cannot start. (CMP-011)

  • Given a change with no rollback plan, then CP-07 denies it and links to the change.

7.12Cross-cutting: shell, business models, geography, Opstral AI, search

Purpose. The capabilities every SynapseDC screen shares: the shell it runs in (menu, breadcrumbs, pinned bars, embedding), the business-model and geography switches that change vocabulary, data and money everywhere, the Opstral AI copilot, global search, and the info bubble on every widget. Lifecycle stage: Plan, Build and Operate. Primary users: every role; product and platform teams for configuration. Builds on: Platform Engineering (identity, feature flags, customisation), AI Foundry (models for the copilot), the platform portal (embedding). Colocation: vocabulary Tenant / cabinet / cage / cross-connect; capacity in kW; the GPU Fleet menu item and site tab are hidden; the site page has a Tenants tab. NeoCloud: vocabulary Customer / GPU cluster / job; capacity in GPUs; GPU Fleet shown; the Command Center's first area is GPU fleet.

7.12.1Scope

In scope

  • Left menu in groups (Operations, Plan & Build, Security, Admin, Reference) with sub-menus per screen; top bar with breadcrumbs, persona and geography switches, global search, Opstral AI.
  • Business model (persona) and geography as first-class settings that regenerate vocabulary, customers, service types, money and the capacity unit everywhere.
  • Opstral AI copilot: natural-language questions answered from the read models with deep links.
  • Global search (⌘K) across sites, devices, customers, services, incidents, changes.
  • Info bubble on every widget; pinned tab bars and list headers; big-screen scaling; embedding with shell=false.
  • Deployment configuration: default persona, persona / geography overrides for embedded use.

Out of scope

  • Identity, roles and SSO (Platform Engineering).
  • Custom fields and tags as records (Platform Engineering Customization); SynapseDC evaluates them over the estate (DCIM 7.10).
  • Model hosting and training (AI Foundry).

7.12.2Business rules

IDRule
BRULE-XC-01The business model is set by, in order: the URL ?persona=, a deployment override, the viewer's saved choice, the deployment default. A saved choice made under a different deployment default is dropped once so returning viewers see the new default.
BRULE-XC-02The geography is set by ?geo= or the saved choice (in India, us USA); it decides currency, number locale, date style, regions, map bounds, place names, people, carbon factor and tariffs.
BRULE-XC-03Changing persona or geography regenerates the whole estate view and every open screen refetches; the physical estate (sites, power, cooling, alarms, incidents) is the same, the vocabulary, customers, service types, room names and money differ.
BRULE-XC-04Money is written in the base currency and converted by the geography's rate; formatting always goes through the geography's formatter (₹ Cr / L, $ K / M); the currency symbol never appears as a literal in a screen.
BRULE-XC-05Every screen URL carries its state (tab, filters, selected object, scope) so a link reproduces the view; new URL parameters are added to the embed guide.
BRULE-XC-06?shell=false hides the menu and top bar for an iframe and is remembered for that tab / frame; ?shell=true shows them; with neither, the shell is shown unless the frame already chose.
BRULE-XC-07Opstral AI answers only from the read models (deterministic, grounded in the data on screen) and every answer links to the screen that holds the figure; it never invents a number.
BRULE-XC-08Every widget carries an info bubble of 1–2 sentences: what it shows, how the number is computed, what clicking does, in the persona's vocabulary.
BRULE-XC-09From 2200 px the whole UI scales (1.25× from 2200, 1.5× from 2880, 2× from 3440) with charts, maps, tooltips and 3D aligned.
BRULE-XC-10Tab bars, list toolbars and grid headers stay pinned under the top bar while content scrolls; nothing is clipped at 1280 or 1440 px.

7.12.3Functional requirements

IDRequirementPriorityModelPhase
XC-001The system shall present a left menu grouped Operations · Plan & Build · Security · Admin · Reference, with sub-menus under the open screen covering its tabs, and breadcrumbs that include the sub-menu.MBothP1
XC-002The system shall offer a business-model switch (network, colocation, cloud, enterprise, NeoCloud) and a geography switch (India, USA) in the top bar, remembered per viewer, honouring ?persona= and ?geo= on load.MBothP1

7.12.4User stories and acceptance criteria

US-XC-01 As a NeoCloud operator opening a link from the platform portal, I want the screen to open in my business model and currency without the menu, so that it fits inside the portal (XC-002, XC-004, XC-011).

  • Given the portal frames /gpu?shell=false&persona=neocloud&geo=us, when the page loads, then no menu or top bar is shown, GPU Fleet opens, money is in $ and the viewer is signed in silently.
  • Given the deployment override persona=neocloud, when a link carries ?persona=colocation, then NeoCloud still opens and the in-app switch still works until the next reload.

US-XC-02 As an operations head, I want to ask "Which issue is costing the most revenue?" and be taken there, so that I don't search through lists (XC-008).

  • Given open issues with money, when I ask in Opstral AI, then the answer names the issue, its lost-to-date and credits exposure, and a link opens the Revenue impact drawer for it.
  • Given no open impact, then the answer says so and links to the 30-day history.

US-XC-03 As a new user, I want every widget to explain itself, so that I don't need training to read a number (XC-009).

  • Given any panel, KPI or tile, when I click its "i", then a bubble explains what it shows, how it is computed and what clicking does, in my model's words.

US-XC-04 As a colocation operator, I want the product to speak of tenants, cabinets and cross-connects everywhere, so that it reads like my business (XC-003, XC-005).

  • Given persona colocation, when I open any screen, then customers are "Tenants", capacity is in kW, GPU Fleet is absent and the site page shows a Tenants tab with cross-connect orders and remote hands.

US-XC-06 As a NOC on a video wall, I want the Command Center readable on a 4K TV, so that the room sees it (XC-012).

  • Given a 3440 px screen, when the Command Center opens, then the UI is scaled 2× and the map, tooltips and charts align with the pointer.

8Analytics-only pages over existing modules

These pages add datacenter analytics on top of records held by existing platform modules. SynapseDC stores none of these records. It reads them (EW / NB-04), derives the measures and drills down into the owning module's screens. Every page is Phase P2, for Both models unless stated. Measures must reconcile with the owning module's own counts.

8.1Programs & Projects insights

Purpose. A portfolio dashboard of datacenter build projects and programmes. Delivery is recorded in the existing Programs & Projects module; this page shows health, cost and schedule across them.

ANL-001The system shall show the widgets above, read from Programs & Projects, without storing project records.MBothP2
ANL-002The system shall mark a project late when its forecast finish is after its target or SPI < 0.90, at risk when CPI < 0.95 or SPI < 0.97, else on track; a programme is red with a late project, amber with one at risk, else green. An approved project not yet started is scheduled no earlier than today.MBothP2
ANL-003The system shall compute EV = budget × progress, CPI and SPI from the module's figures.MBothP2
ANL-004The system shall link each project to its source Planner plan (one plan per project).SBothP2
ANL-005The system shall show capacity coming online as GPUs for NeoCloud and as cabinets / kW for colocation.SBothP2
ANL-006The system shall show change orders with their approver by size: ≤ 5 % of budget the project manager, ≤ 15 % the programme sponsor, else the investment committee; an approved change order moves the approved budget and the finish.SBothP2
ANL-007The system shall rank project risks by likelihood × impact and show derived risks (late POs, permits not granted within 6 weeks, CPI < 0.97, SPI < 0.95 or finish after target, waiting on a predecessor).SBothP2
ANL-008The system shall show programme start gates: a project waits until its predecessors are delivered, and long-lead MEP orders at kick-off for greenfield phases.SBothP2
ANL-009The system shall show work orders by state (Planned, Ready, Awaiting change, In progress, Done) and kit (Ready, Partial, Not ordered), and never show a work order of a project that has not started as more than Ready.MBothP2

Data needed from Programs & Projects: programmes (dependencies, phases), projects (stage, start, finish, target, budget approved, progress, planned progress, earned, actual), milestones, work orders (state, kit, crew, change request), commissioning tests, permits, design documents, risks, change orders, the planner plan id. From Supply Chain: purchase orders (supplier, lines, ordered, due, state, value).

8.2Work orders and process KPIs

Purpose. A view of field work and the twelve datacenter processes across the estate, with targets: P01 Site onboarding, P02 Hall fit-out, P03 Customer onboarding, P04 Major incident, P05 Change, P06 Capacity expansion, P07 Firmware campaign, P08 Decommission, P09 Reconciliation, P10 Work order, P11 Maintenance mode, P12 Access request. Each has lanes (candidate roles), user steps with hour targets and automatic steps. The process definitions and instances run over the existing records (delivery work orders = P10, contractor visits = P12, open changes = P05, maintenance = P11, Critical / High incidents = P04, projects = P02 / P03 / P07 / P08, sites being built = P01); the process engine and its instances belong to the existing modules (Programs & Projects, Field Force Management); SynapseDC reads them through their APIs and runs no process engine of its own.

ANL-033The system shall show differences between IP Management and the connected DNS / DHCP / IPAM systems.SBothP2
ANL-034The system shall show InfiniBand and front-end fabric address space per GPU pod.SNeoP2
ANL-035The system shall show each site's management space as its own VRF (sites may reuse management ranges) under one container, and the fabric plan per site (loopbacks, point-to-point /31s, a /22 per hall, storage).SBothP2

Data needed from IP Management: VRFs, prefixes (parent, size, role, site), addresses (device, interface, primary), VLANs, ASNs, connector differences. From Inventory: device install dates.

8.5Change management overview

Purpose. A datacenter view of every change, maintenance and delivery change request in Services & ITSM Change Management. It covers volume, outcome, lead time, approvals, freezes and which changes caused incidents.

WidgetMeasure / formulaSource moduleDrill-down
Volume and outcomechanges per week by class (Standard / Normal / Emergency) and outcome; failed includes "caused an incident"Change Managementlist
Lead timerequest → approval → start, per classChange Managementchange
Open by stageDraft / Pending / Approved / In progressChange Managementlist
Approvals and the boardapprovals waiting by role (change manager, site lead from Medium risk, CAB at High, ECAB for Emergency)Change Managementapproval
Next 14 days / calendarwindows; conflicts (overlapping windows on the same devices); freezes (open Critical incident at a site; peak and year-end calendar)Change Management + SynapseDC incidentschange
By site / where changes failcount and failure % by site, source and templateChange Managementfiltered list
Caused an incidenta completed change cited by an incident's leading root-cause hypothesisSynapseDC RCA + Change Managementincident / change
IDRequirementPriorityModelPhase
ANL-040The system shall show the overview widgets in equal-height pairs, with a tabbed detail table (needs attention, waiting for review, next 14 days, last 28 days).MBothP2
ANL-041The system shall count a change as failed when it rolled back or was cited by an incident's leading hypothesis.MBothP2
ANL-042The system shall show site freezes derived from open Critical incidents, alongside the calendar freezes.MBothP2
ANL-043The system shall flag conflicting windows on the same devices at the same site.SBothP2
ANL-044The system shall show policy checks on changes: POL-01 no rollback plan and POL-02 inside a freeze = Block; POL-03 high-risk window outside 22:00–06:00 (not emergency or delivery changes), POL-04 touches a single point of failure (not standard changes), POL-05 overlapping window on the same devices, POL-06 post-implementation review overdue on a completed emergency change = Warn.SBothP2
ANL-046The system shall apply freezes as the module does: a site with an open Critical incident allows emergency changes only; peak-season and year-end calendar freezes allow standard and emergency changes; freezes hold live sites only and never civil works.MBothP2
ANL-047The system shall measure lead time on normal changes implemented each week: median days from raised to the last approval, then from approval to the window.SBothP2
ANL-048The system shall include preventive maintenance done in the last 12 weeks as completed standard changes (template STD-03) in volume and outcome.SBothP2
ANL-045The system shall include DCIM-raised changes and delivery change requests in all measures.MBothP2

Data needed from Change Management: changes (class, risk, status, window, devices, site, approvals, rollback, origin, template), approval matrix, calendar freezes, standard templates. From SynapseDC: incidents and RCA hypotheses.

8.6DCIM Insights

DCIM Insights is specified with DCIM (7.10, DCIM-034). Its widgets are:

  • needs attention (the same filters as the Racks, Cabling, Power and Devices lists);
  • sites and halls by power pressure (85 % marker);
  • rack density;
  • device donuts;
  • cabling stacks;
  • power panels by load;
  • makers;
  • change requests.

It reads DCIM extensions and Inventory records only.

9Integration with the existing platform modules

Every module below is consumed through its API only (ARCH-001…007); the API is assumed available and SynapseDC builds nothing of the module itself. The module is the system of record for the objects listed under "SynapseDC reads". SynapseDC never writes those objects except where "SynapseDC sends" says so, and then through the module's own API or the platform event bus, under the module team's contract. Interface references are to the interface specification (NB = northbound, INT = southbound / records, EW = east–west). Each row is a requirement: INTG-nnn.

9.11Expected API responses — how to read this part

For every API SynapseDC consumes (section 3.5), this appendix gives the call, who calls it, the request, an example of the expected response and the fields SynapseDC needs (M = must, O = optional). Paths are illustrative: each module team owns its real API, and an adapter in SynapseDC maps the real response onto these fields. A field a module cannot provide goes to the API gap list (ARCH-004) — SynapseDC never builds a local substitute. Times are RFC 3339 UTC; ids are the module's stable ids; money is { amount, currency }.

The sections that follow give one module each: 9.12 OAP telemetry · 9.13 System Alerts · 9.14 Security Ops · 9.15 Telemetry / Data / Infra / Fin Ops · 9.16 Services & ITSM · 9.17 AI Foundry · 9.18 BSS · 9.19 Inventory · 9.20 Inventory Planning · 9.21 IP Management · 9.22 Programs & Projects · 9.23 Supply Chain · 9.24 Platform Engineering · 9.25 Intelligence Hub. Section 9.26 lists every write SynapseDC makes into these modules.

9.12Telemetry — OAP ingestion / consumer services (ClickHouse)

Module: OAP telemetry ingestion and consumer microservices, on ClickHouse. SynapseDC uses it for every live figure, 24 h trend, history and freshness. Screens: Command Center, Datacenters (Metrics, Equipment, layout overlays), Facilities, GPU Fleet, Capacity and power economics, device page.

Paths are illustrative — the module team owns the real API; an adapter in SynapseDC maps the real response to these fields. A missing field goes to the API gap list (ARCH-004). SynapseDC never queries ClickHouse directly (ARCH-009); every read goes through the consumer API, every write through the ingestion API.

Request shape used by API-OAP-01…03. A query names one metric, a label match and the time scope. It is neutral about the query language (PromQL, SQL or the service's own DSL).

{ "metric": "dco_gpu_sm_active_ratio",
  "match": { "site": "TX-DC-020", "cluster": "CL-TX-020-A" },
  "start": "2026-10-27T09:00:00Z", "end": "2026-10-28T09:00:00Z", "step": "1h",
  "agg": { "fn": "avg", "by": ["device"] } }

9.19Plan to Build — Inventory

Module: Plan to Build Inventory. SynapseDC uses it as the record of every location, rack and device, their components and lifecycle; it reads them by id and writes back only the DCIM fields Inventory can carry. It is used on the Datacenters list and site page, device page, Facilities (rack power, PDUs), DCIM, the twins, GPU Fleet (node → rack), Capacity and the Planner's site baseline.

API-INV-01 Locations

Method · illustrative pathDirectionCalled byRate / trigger

API-INV-07 Write DCIM extensions and observed state

Method · illustrative pathDirectionCalled byRate / trigger
PATCH /assets/{id}, PATCH /racks/{id}, POST /assets/{id}/observationsOutDCIM edits under a change request (DCIM-032), collector discovery reconciliation (DATA-013)on each DCIM write; observations every 6–24 h

Request body (extension):

{ "changeRequest": "CHG-2311", "fields": { "u": 14, "uSize": 2, "rackId": "RACK-B09", "status": "In service" } }

Request body (observation):

{ "observedAt": "2026-10-27T03:00:00Z", "source": "collector-IL-DC-009-a",
  "serial": "FDO2301A0ZQ", "model": "N9K-C93180YC-FX", "os": "NX-OS 10.3(4a)",
  "modules": [ { "slot": "PSU2", "serial": "LIT2303", "state": "Fault" } ],
  "lldp": [ { "localPort": "Eth1/49", "remoteDevice": "SPINE-01", "remotePort": "Eth1/7" } ] }

Expected response: 200 { "id": "SW-LEAF-03", "version": 42, "drift": [ { "attr": "os", "record": "NX-OS 10.2(5)", "observed": "NX-OS 10.3(4a)" } ] }.

DCIM extension fields SynapseDC would like Inventory to carry (OD-01). Each one Inventory accepts is written here; each one it does not stays in DC-STORE, keyed by the Inventory id:

FieldOnNotes
u, uSizedeviceU position and height
row, gridPosition (col, row), facerackfloor plan and the twin
rackType (e.g. RT-TS8-42-1000), condition (OK / Damaged at audit + note)rackvintage and replacement plans
pduSide A / B, pduId, outlet per PSUdevice power portpower chain, single-side SPOF
powerPanel (busway A / B per hall, −48 V), feed (V, A, phases, rating kW)rack PDUpanel load, feed rating
patchPanel, trunk, odfPort, crossConnectcable pathstructured cabling hops
sensorPosition (rack, height)sensorrack inlet heat map

Stays in DC-STORE whatever Inventory decides: work items under a change request, U reservations, cooling coverage (nearest unit, liquid share), facility design ratings (site design kW, UPS / DG nameplate, tank, redundancy) where Inventory has no field.

FieldTypeReq.Used for
id, versionstring / intMoptimistic concurrency (If-Match)
drift[]arrayOdrift shown on device and DCIM

9.23Supply Chain

Module: Materials, Vendors, Contracts, Procurement, Warehouses, Field Force Management. SynapseDC uses it for support contracts, catalogue prices and lead times, spares, purchase orders from plans and field visits. It is used on site Assets (lifecycle, RMA, spares), the Planner (sourcing, Materials, pack), the Access tab (contractor visits) and Work orders.

9.26Write-back: what the SynapseDC UI updates in the existing modules

SynapseDC does not only read the existing modules. Many actions on its screens update them, and some updates happen automatically. Every update goes through the module's write API (ARCH-007), so the module applies its own validation, approvals and audit. The tables below list every write: first the user actions screen by screen, then the automatic writes. Expected requests and responses are in 9.12–9.25.

Rules for every write

IDRule
WR-001A user action is sent on behalf of the signed-in user (their platform token, API-PEN-01), so the module records the real actor. Automatic writes use the SynapseDC service account and carry the reason (rule, incident, plan).
WR-002Every write carries an Idempotency-Key; an update of an existing record sends If-Match with the version the user saw. A 409 / 412 reloads the record and shows what changed.
WR-003Inventory and IP Management writes from DCIM and the Planner carry a change request (standard, open or new; DCIM-020). Without one the write is not sent.
WR-004The module's answer wins. When it refuses a write (validation, approval, freeze, permission), SynapseDC shows the module's message and saves nothing locally; there is no optimistic local copy (ARCH-003).
WR-005After a successful write SynapseDC refreshes from the module (response body, then the module event), never from its own guess of the result.
WR-006Automatic writes retry with back-off (1 min, 5 min, 30 min, then hourly for 24 h). A write still failing raises a Data Ops alarm (NFR-017) and shows "not synced" on the record.
WR-007Every write is in the SynapseDC audit trail with the module, API id, the module's record id and version, the actor and the change request.
WR-008Datacenter-specific data (RCA feedback, action steps, plans, compliance, U reservations, power contracts) is written to DC-STORE, not to a module. These rows are marked DC-STORE below.

A. User actions that update a module, screen by screen

10Data requirements

10.1Entity model

51 entities in 107 relationships, in nine domains: Estate & locations · Facility (power, cooling, environment & safety) · Equipment & connectivity · GPU · Customers & services · Operations (alarms, incidents, changes, problems, on-call) · Lifecycle & process · Access & security · Planning & delivery. Every field is classified Import (a record someone enters or a module syncs), Live (a reading or event from the collector) or Derived (computed by a read model; never stored as input).

IDRequirementPriorityPhase
DATA-001Every count on every screen shall be derived from entities (devices, interfaces, links, services, alarms, incidents); no count is typed in or configured.MP1
DATA-002Every entity shall have a stable string identity; external references (ITSM number, CRM id, DCIM id, PACS badge id) are kept in externalRefs[], never used as the key.MP1
DATA-003Every live sample shall keep the source timestamp and the receipt timestamp; a value is fresh while the last sample is younger than two polling intervals, else stale; no successful poll for 15 min → device unmonitored.MP1
DATA-004Record data shall carry the last successful sync time and the system of record.MP1
DATA-005Both the normalised alarm and the raw source event shall be stored for audit and replay.MP1
DATA-006Money shall be stored as amount + currency in the operator's currency; tariffs and benchmarks carry an "illustrative" flag until replaced by the customer's values.MP1
DATA-007The containment chain region → site → floor → room → row → rack → device → port shall be complete for every live device; a device without a rack is a data-quality finding.MP1
DATA-008Tenant-owned equipment (Colo) shall be stored as an opaque block per cabinet (owner, contracted kW, role) with no device records inside it.MP1
DATA-009GPU records shall carry the DCGM field set per GPU and 24 h history; job records carry job id, customer, GPUs / nodes, state, step time, MFU, last checkpoint. Workload data, models and code are never stored.MP1
DATA-010Custom fields and tags from Platform Engineering shall be attachable to site, rack, device and customer.SP2
DATA-015Telemetry samples shall be stored only in ClickHouse through the OAP ingestion service; SynapseDC stores no samples, and every live figure is read through the OAP consumer API with its source and receipt time.MP1
DATA-016Records owned by an existing module shall be referenced by that module's id; SynapseDC holds only derived results and datacenter-specific extensions keyed by it (ARCH-003, ARCH-012).MP1
DATA-011All per-persona vocabulary (customer / service / premium words, capacity unit, money words) shall be configuration, not code.MP1

10.2Onboarding data (the import template)

The inventory import template (one sheet per entity, 35 data sheets, field dictionary, lookups, summary checks) is the fallback for every record no system holds: physical placement, power chain, structured cabling, design ratings (site design kW, rack budgets, UPS / DG nameplate, tank, cooling capacity, redundancy, Tier), sensor positions, commercial records, service paths, lifecycle dates for vendors without an API, process records (PM plan, inspections, tests, risks, spares minimums, on-call rota). Load order: Sites → Floors → Rooms → Racks → power / cooling plant → Devices → Interfaces → Links → GPU → Customers → Services → Collectors. Core data is required before a site goes live; Recommended before Facilities and Revenue are trusted; Optional later.

IDRequirementPriorityPhase
DATA-012The system shall import the template with per-sheet validation (lookups, references, U clashes, rack budget, Σ checks) and report every rejected row with its reason.MP1
DATA-013The system shall reconcile an imported site against the collector's discovered inventory (serial, model, modules, LLDP) and report differences to the inventory module; it shall not resolve them itself.MP1
DATA-014The system shall export any site in the same template layout (data backup).SP2

10.3Retention

Telemetry retention (raw samples and roll-ups) is set in the OAP telemetry services on ClickHouse; the figures below are what SynapseDC's screens need from them.

DataRetention (proposed, to discuss)
Raw samples30 days
5-minute roll-ups13 months
Hourly roll-ups5 years
Alarms, incidents, changes, problems5 years
Access events1 year; CCTV references 90 days
Audit trail7 years

11Non-functional requirements

IDAreaRequirementPriority
NFR-001AvailabilityCentral platform API and screens 99.9 % monthly; the collector buffers 72 h so a WAN outage leaves no gap.M
NFR-002LatencyTrap / push sources on screen and in the northbound event ≤ 15 s p95; polled sources ≤ one interval + 10 s; incident opened ≤ 60 s after the first alarm; API p95 ≤ 300 ms (one object), ≤ 1 s (lists and roll-ups).M
NFR-003Scale100 sites, 200 k devices, 50 k GPUs per tenant; ≥ 500 k samples / s and ≥ 2 k events / s per region. The sample rate is carried by the OAP telemetry ingestion service on ClickHouse (a dependency); SynapseDC's own load is the read models and API.M
NFR-004PollingUPS / ATS 10 s + traps; interfaces 30 s (gNMI 10 s); ICMP 30 s, 3 misses; DCGM 10–30 s; PDUs / sensors / cooling / BMC 60 s; inventory 6–24 h + events; records webhooks + daily; OEM weekly.M
NFR-005SecurityRead-only southbound; outbound-only collector over mTLS 443; SNMPv3 authPriv; Redfish HTTPS; BACnet/SC where available; Modbus / BACnet/IP / SNMP v2c only on isolated VLANs; credentials in the collector vault, rotated ≤ 90 days; OAuth 2.0 / OIDC northbound with scopes per area; every write audited.M
NFR-006Tenancy and privacyPer-tenant separation; tenant-scoped tokens see only their objects; colocation tenant gear never polled; GPU workload contents never read; data residency per customer.M
NFR-007ReconciliationEvery roll-up equals the sum of its parts (alarms in scope, incidents, services, money, capacity); automated checks run on every data change.M
NFR-008FreshnessEvery live figure shows its freshness; stale and unmonitored states are visible, never hidden.M
NFR-009UsabilityNST / Vision Waves design system; no secondary left menus inside screens; every widget carries an info bubble (what it shows, how computed, what clicking does); 1280 and 1440 layouts with no horizontal overflow; TV scaling from 2200 px.M
NFR-010EmbeddingAny screen runs inside the platform portal (shell=false) with silent SSO; deep links keep their parameters.M
NFR-011AccessibilityWCAG 2.1 AA for colour contrast, keyboard navigation and focus; status never by colour alone.S
NFR-012LocalisationGeography sets currency, number and date formats; India and USA first.M
NFR-013Big screensFrom 2200 px the UI scales (1.25 / 1.5 / 2×) with charts, maps and 3D aligned.S
NFR-0143DRealistic twin degrades automatically on slow GPUs (post-processing dropped); schematic twin works without WebGL via the 2D fallback.S
NFR-015AuditabilityEvery API write, record change, integration call and AI answer is in the audit trail with actor, before / after and change request.M
NFR-016RecoveryRPO 5 min, RTO 1 h for the central platform.S
NFR-017Observability of integrationsEvery connector reports last success, error count and lag; failures raise an alarm under Data Ops.M
NFR-018APIOpenAPI 3.1 generated from the contract; cursor paging; ETag / If-Match; Idempotency-Key on POST; RFC 9457 errors; rate limits 600 reads / 60 writes per minute per client; deprecation ≥ 6 months.M
NFR-019EventsCloudEvents 1.0; at-least-once with id and per-subject sequence; webhook HMAC signature and retry schedule; Kafka topics versioned.M
NFR-020TestingEach screen has an automated layout and data check (overflow, NaN / undefined, equal heights, sticky headers); each read model has a reconciliation check; each interface has contract tests and failure drills (source down, WAN down, receiver down, duplicates).M

12.1Onboarding a customer site (the delivery approach)

Each site follows the same onboarding journey; the BRD requirements each step relies on are listed.
